
# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code when working with code in this repository.

## Project Overview

E2E test suite for the **Therapios** platform using Playwright. Therapios is a German healthcare management platform for physiotherapy practices. Tests cover three user roles — Admin, Therapist, and SuperAdmin — against both Staging and Production environments.

**Platform language:** German (UI labels, field names, and status values are in German)

## Commands

There are no custom npm scripts — run Playwright directly:

```bash
# Run all tests for a specific role (Staging)
npx playwright test --project=AdminJhen          # Staging Admin
npx playwright test --project=SandraZeibig       # Staging Therapist
npx playwright test --project=SAJhen             # Staging SuperAdmin

# Run all tests for a specific role (Production)
npx playwright test --project=AdminJhen-Prod     # Production Admin
npx playwright test --project=JhenQA-Prod        # Production Therapist
npx playwright test --project=SAJhen-Prod        # Production SuperAdmin

# Run a single spec file
npx playwright test tests/Staging/Admin/admin_search.spec.ts --project=AdminJhen

# Run by tag (mix and match role + feature tags)
npx playwright test --project=AdminJhen --grep "@CRMActivities"
npx playwright test --project=SAJhen --grep "@Abrechnung"
npx playwright test --project=SandraZeibig --grep "@uploadvo"

# Run headed (visible browser)
npx playwright test --project=AdminJhen --headed

# Re-generate auth state (do this when sessions expire)
npx playwright test --project=setup             # Staging auth
npx playwright test --project=setup-prod        # Production auth

# View HTML report
npx playwright show-report
```

| Project | Role | Environment | Auth file |
|---|---|---|---|
| `setup` | — | Staging | generates all Staging `.auth` files |
| `AdminJhen` | Admin | Staging | `.auth/AdminJhen.json` |

| `JhenQA-Prod` | Therapist | Production | `.auth/JhenQA-Prod.json` |

- **Production base URL**: `https://app.therapios.de/`

Repro VO 1434-32: stored `homeVisit` true, check **50 `home_visit_expected_for_care_facility`** passed, form toggle on and "Hausbesuch" absent from the FEHLGESCHLAGEN list; control VO 4823-5 (facility, no HBH-*, toggle off) still fails with the German message; a sweep of 716 non-closed VOs finds 619 with an HBH-* remedy + Einrichtung and **0** with the flag off (AC3's population); the for_fixing queue is used to compare stored verdicts against the toggle. Traps: the check is id 50, not id 3 `home_visit_marked`; the facility is `elderlyCareHome`, not `entity`; `homeVisit`/code/facility API filters are silently ignored. AC3/AC4 backfill → `fixme` (console command). Uses `Pages/admin/admin.vo-validation.page.ts` |
| `admin_scan_selbstzahler_prefill.spec.ts` | `@Admin @ScanInsurancePrefill @ReadOnly @Slow` | RC 3.14 (#3710, commit `bbee3b6a5`) a "Selbstzahler" scan must stop pre-filling Privat Basis: the patient's own stored insurance type wins, the field is left empty with a hint when they have none, and a Blanko VO on a Privat Basis patient gets a second hint. **DEPLOYED 2026-09-17 — 7 passed, 2 `fixme`.** **This ticket is frontend-only, so `GET /status` cannot answer for it at all:** `bbee3b6a5` is an ancestor of `release/3.14.0`, the API reports `3.14.0`, and for a day both new i18n keys and both German strings occurred **0 times in the served bundle** while #3383's sibling key in the same dictionary section occurred **4** — the control that makes a zero mean something. The deployment test re-derives that verdict every run and the AC tests are gated on it, so the file flipped to asserting the fix with no edit when the bundle shipped (`entry-01bd9372…`, keys ×3, both German strings present). **Do NOT probe the bundle for `isSelfPayerScan`** — minification renames a function, so 0 proves nothing either way; keys and string literals survive. **The before/after is on record from one unchanged test:** image **10589** (a Selbstzahler scan for **Katharina Overath, stored PKV**) pre-filled **"Privat Basis"** on the pre-fix build, with the form flagging its own guess via #3383's badge *"Weicht von der Versicherung des Patienten ab"* — Flow telling the admin on the same screen that the value it chose disagreed with the patient's record — and pre-fills **"PKV"** with no badge on the deployed build. **The rule is a pure function, so both versions are ported and the painted value is compared against BOTH** (the #3704 dual-oracle technique), which is what lets each test assert the right thing on either build instead of needing a rewrite on deploy. **That also disqualifies the obvious fixture:** a Selbstzahler scan on a patient already stored `privat_basis` gives "Privat Basis" under either rule — 3 of the 9 candidates are like that, so a QA who picks one sees the correct value on a broken build; only a patient stored **PKV** separates them (6 images / 4 patients: 10310, 10589, 12132, 12133, 12134, 12650). **Fixture verdict over the whole 987-image pending-review pool:** AC1 **yes** (9 self-payer scans, 6 discriminating); **AC2 no** — all 9 match a patient who already has a stored type, so the empty-field case cannot be produced; AC3 yes but **not discriminating** (every named-insurer scan sits on a patient already stored PKV, so scan-wins and patient-wins agree); **AC4 yes and discriminating** — images **12342 / 12768** are BG scans on GKV patients, the one unaffected-path fixture that can actually fail if the change leaks past `privat_basis` (verified: the form paints BG, not GKV); **AC5 no** — all 8 Blanko scans are on GKV patients. **The implementation is wider than the Developer Reference predicted:** it names only `insurancePrefill.ts`, but `mapExtractionToFormValues.ts` needed the same guard — the mapper runs FIRST and writes `overrides.insuranceType` from the scan, so without it the resolver's new branch would be defeated by an already-filled field. **Traps:** `extractedData.insuranceType.value` is the **raw AI text** on older rows and the mapped enum on newer ones (`'Selbstzahler'` 7, `'Privat'` 2, `'GKV'` 330, `'public'` 117, `'PKV'` 11, `'private'` 6, `'BG'` 2, `'PKVH'` 1, none 511) — **`privat_basis` never appears in stored data**, so an API grep for it finds zero and reads as "no Selbstzahler scans exist" when there are nine; **`/patients?insuranceType=` is silently IGNORED** (every value returns all 8,384); the collection does **not** serialize `extractedData` and `groups[]` is ignored there, so the sweep costs one item read per image; and `getUsableScannedInsuranceType()` additionally gates on **confidence**, a third branch no AC mentions — an unusable confidence makes the scan contribute nothing, so neither the pre-fill nor the hint fires. **Two notes for the PM:** the ticket's Localization Reference records both German strings as "Pending", but the commit supplied them (`"Selbstzahler erkannt: bitte PKV oder Privat Basis wählen."` / `"Blanko-VOs gelten möglicherweise nicht für Privat Basis. Bitte den Heilmittel-Typ prüfen."`); and **AC5's shipped condition is a superset** — the hint keys on the Blanko switch's live value rather than on the scan having read a Blanko form, so manually toggling Blanko on a Privat Basis patient warns too, which is also the cheapest manual route to test it. AC2/AC5 → `fixme` on **two** independent walls each (not deployed, and no fixture), each recorded separately so the right one can be cleared first. **Read-only** — the VO form is opened and read, never saved. Uses `Pages/admin/admin.scan-insurance-prefill.page.ts` |
| `admin_crm_absence_indicator.spec.ts` | `@Admin @CRMAbsenceIndicator @Security @ReadOnly` | RC 3.15 (#3721, PR #3782 merged into `release/3.15.0` 2026-09-23) a coloured dot on the CRM practice detail's two VO lists says that nothing is happening because the therapist is away — **RED** on **Nachverfolgen** when they are absent today, **AMBER** on **Bestellen** when an absence falls inside the practice's ordering lead-time window. **Deployed; all five ACs verified, 7 passed, 0 `fixme`. Read-only** — every request a GET, which is also **AC5** (the indicator is informational; the practice's Bestellen list is snapshotted before and after and nothing moves). Imports `test` from `tests/fixtures/crm-serial`. **PERSONAL-DATA DISCIPLINE, which the feature itself sets the standard for and the spec mirrors.** The new `GET /therapist-absence-indicators?practice=&therapist[]=` deliberately returns **two booleans and nothing else**, so “Krankheit on Thursday” about a named employee never reaches a CRM list — and that shape is **asserted**: the payload carries exactly `therapistId` / `absentToday` / `absentInLeadTimeWindow`, no date and no absence type. The independent oracle reads `/absence-days` for **dates only**, never the type, and no assertion or log line puts a date against a person. **Three deployment properties, each worth its own check:** the route answers against a **404 control**; it is **kebab-case**, so `/therapist_absence_indicators` — the form every other collection uses — **404s and reads exactly like “not deployed”** (the `/absence-days` trap, #3394); and it **fails closed**, answering **400** with no parameters rather than a full roster. **AC1/AC3 are re-derived from `/absence-days`** over a 12-therapist sample deliberately spanning all three combinations — **28 absent today / 25 in-window-only / 31 neither** of 84 — with 12/12 agreement. **The in-window-only group IS AC2's discriminating case**: a therapist who genuinely has an absence, just not today, which a naive “has any absence” check turns red for. **The window length is falsifiable from the data, which I expected not to be:** over all 84 therapists, nearest-upcoming-absence **within 21d → flagged 53, no upcoming absence → false 17, and BEYOND 21d → false 14** — **0 disagreements**, and those 14 are what rules out “any future absence counts”. Plus the structural invariant that **`absentToday` implies `absentInLeadTimeWindow`** (today is inside the window), which holds on all 28. **Authorization** is the whole of its access control, since it answers about OTHER PEOPLE by construction and has no self-service case: **therapist 403, admin 200, super admin 200** — asserted as status codes, never by reading the data. **On screen** (practice 633, which holds both lists for the same absent therapist): **6 red dots `rgb(211, 47, 47)`** on Nachverfolgung and **11 amber `rgb(230, 167, 0)`** on Bestellung, each with its own `accessibilityLabel` (`Therapeut:in heute abwesend` / `…in der Bestellvorlaufzeit abwesend`) — and **the other tone asserted ABSENT on each tab**, because a dot of the wrong colour is invisible to a count of the right one. **AC2 on screen has a live discriminating fixture**: therapist **24** is on the same page with **84 absence days** and is NOT absent today, so the undotted rows are exactly the case the naive check fails. **The batching AC is read off the request**: **ONE** `/therapist-absence-indicators` call per rendered tab, naming 2 therapists for 11 rows — a practice's list repeats the same handful of therapists, so per-row would be an N+1 for an identical answer. **Two coverage gaps measured rather than assumed**, both reported: AC1/AC3 say a **half day** counts, and **every absence row on staging is `FULL`** (the standing #3394/#3663 finding), so that clause is unexercised; and **every practice is 21d/Standard** (#3302), so a provider that ignored `practice.leadTimeDays` and hardcoded the standard would pass here — which is exactly why the PR's own boundary test uses a 10-day practice. Uses `Pages/crm/crm.absence-indicator.page.ts` |

Datum`** where the Reference writes *VO number*, *Patient* and *Ausstellungsdatum*. The earlier AC6 test only asserted the HEADER exists, which is how the defect passed it — the new test compares VALUES, with an anti-vacuity guard requiring a dated BG/no-type row. **Two test traps fixed:** the table is **column-major in the DOM** (the VO-number column is its own frozen stack), so text after `[100038-1]` is the next column's HEADER, not the row — cells are matched by y-overlap under the `Gültig bis` header; and the AC7 check read `tabVos()`, which sends **no `order` key** and returns id order (ids 100132+ issued 02.10 sit before 100017 issued 01.09), failing on a correct tab — it now reads the PAINTED order, and the captured request carries `order[date]=asc`. | `admin_cross_area_fee_validation.spec.ts` | `@Admin @CrossAreaValidation @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3576) VO creation/edit validation must flag a fee or Heilmittel whose treatment area differs from the VO's own Fachbereich. **Deployed and passing — validation id 54, `fee_area_matches_therapy_type`, severity `warning`, sort 42, category `remedy`, seeded by `Version20260901142128`; 13 tests, 1 `fixme`.** **Read-only, and the reason is the whole method:** `POST /prescriptions/preview-creation-validation` — the endpoint the CREATE form itself calls — hydrates a **transient** Prescription and, in its controller's own words, "does not read or write any PrescriptionValidation rows". So AC2's nine-row truth table is driven **exactly**, including combinations no VO on staging has, from a three-key payload (`therapyType`, `insuranceType`, `prescribedTreatments[].treatment` IRI). Omit `changedFields`: sending it (even `[]`) switches the backend to `evaluateAffected()` and re-runs nothing, which reads as "the check does not exist". Verified: AC2 **18 combinations, 0 mismatches** (the table driven once on a `treatment`-kind Heilmittel per area and once on a fee per area), each warning's German note compared against an independently rebuilt string so a check that flags the right rows but names the wrong ones still fails; AC1 (registered beside the other 20, the only new one, and one of exactly **two** warning-severity creation checks with `icd10_code_valid`); AC5; every mismatched row named and the correctly-aread one left out; `passiv` rows in scope **in both directions** (the implementation deliberately covers every `PrescribedTreatment`, not only `TreatmentKind::TREATMENT` like its sibling checks — AEB/KT-H-BV pin it); an **area-less catalogue row is not a mismatch** (ids 80 `<FEHLER>` and 103 `K` pass silently), nor is a VO with no Fachbereich or no prescribed rows. **The live population is surveyed, not sampled** — every catalogue row against both foreign therapy types, 266 `totalItems` probes: **29 VOs / 34 offending rows** spanning all three Fachbereiche and all four kinds (12 treatment / 11 one-time-fee / 8 per-treatment-fee / 3 passiv), 19 imported. Eight of them are re-evaluated through the form's own endpoint from each VO's OWN Fachbereich and prescribed rows, and all warn with every offending code named — VO **7209-9** (ergotherapy, carrying AB-P + KG + HBH-PT, all PT) is the three-mismatch specimen. **No VO carries a stored verdict for check 54** (7 of 10 sampled have verdicts at all, 0 for this check), because a verdict is only written when a VO is saved or re-checked — which is why AC1 has to be read off the endpoint rather than by hunting for existing amber rows. **THE TRAP, and it silently kills a third of any survey:** `TherapyType` is `physiotherapy` / **`ergotherapy`** / `speech_therapy` — NOT `occupational_therapy`, which `/prescriptions?therapyType=` accepts **silently** and answers `totalItems: 0` for, exactly like a nonsense value (`ergotherapy` answers 9,113). The first test proves the vocabulary partitions before any zero from this endpoint is believed. **Also note `treatment.area` has exactly three values** (PT 79 / ERGO 43 / SSSST 11 of 135, 2 area-less) — the Developer Reference's sub-area worry (`PT_ZAE`, `ET_NUT`, `PO`) is about `remedy_catalog.therapyArea`, a column the check does not read, so there is no roll-up decision to make; and **`SSSST` is five S** — `TreatmentAreaEnum` read `SSST` until this ticket needed it, because nothing had ever compared against the speech-therapy case, so anything that did ask silently answered "not speech therapy". Reading a VO's prescribed rows needs care too: `groups[]` **replaces** the default set (`overrideDefaultGroups: true`), so `groups[]=billing:read` returns `therapyType: null`, and an embedded `treatment` carries `@id` but **no bare `id`**. **Finding** → the check **does not apply to a Privat Basis VO**: #3608 (`Version20260903140041`) gave every PKV check the `privat_basis` insurance type "so the two types always evaluate the same set", and id 54 — seeded two days earlier — is the **only one of the 7 PKV creation checks that did not get it**. Verified behaviourally: the same shape that warns under public/private/accident is **not evaluated at all** under `privat_basis` (6 other checks still run, so it is not an empty set). **AC3/AC4 → `fixme`, a PM question rather than a defect:** severity is display-only. `summarizeStates()` requires `failed === 0` for `allPassed` and `VoForm.handleSave()` submits only when `allPassed`, while the panel merely splits warnings out of the count with the comment "Split out for display only … so saving behaves exactly as before" (#3342) — so a cross-area warning leaves "Speichern" inert exactly as an error does, against AC3's "not a blocking error — the admin can still save" and the migration's own "the check never blocks a save". AC4's acknowledge route (the panel's per-check "Bestanden" toggle, which flips the check and makes `allPassed` true) does work. Not asserted end-to-end because observing the save gate means driving Create-VO to completion on a real patient. Uses `Pages/admin/admin.cross-area-validation.page.ts` |

| `therapist_sync_retry_recovery.spec.ts` | `@Therapist @SyncQueue @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3594) a temporary server problem (a 409 collision with another save, or a 5xx outage) must be retried automatically instead of being filed under "Nicht gespeichert", and the startup recovery notice must count only entries still waiting to send. **Deployed; all seven ACs verified live.** **The ticket declares two of its own cases untestable and both are one-liners here:** AC1 is "hard to provoke by hand … there is no manual staging repro — do not attempt to force one" and AC2 wants an emulator with its DNS pointed at a dead address. But `packages/dataprovider` is shared with the WEB build — the queue persists to `localStorage` under **`dataprovider/queue/v1`** (`{version:1, entries:[{id,resource,type,payload,state,attempts,createdAt,updatedAt,lastError}]}`, states `pending|syncing|retrying|failed|done`) and the replay executor writes to **`POST /activities`** (not `/activities/bulk`), so `page.route()` returns the exact 409/503/201 each AC describes on demand. **Nothing reaches the server:** every write is answered by the test, so no Activity is created — and the queue is SEEDED with `addInitScript` rather than produced through the Doku modal, which makes AC6's "2 waiting and 1 filed" a literal fixture instead of the ticket's six-step offline recipe, and skips the suite's flakiest surface entirely. Verified: AC1/AC2 both trace `pending → retrying → pending` and **never** touch `failed`, with **10** retries the app made on its own and the entry left at `attempts: 0` (`markPending(resetAttempts)`); AC3's two entries reach `done` untouched and the bar goes to "alles gesendet"; AC4 3 pending → "(3)"; AC5 only-filed → **no notice at all**, panel intact with Grund / "Erneut versuchen" / "Verwerfen"; AC6 → **"(2)"**; AC7 422 → `failed` with `reasonKey: sync.failure.validation`. **The trap that decides whether this ticket reads as shipped:** the Developer Reference says "409 currently sits in the permanent set and needs to move to the transient/retry path" — **it never moved.** `PERMANENT_STATUSES` in the served bundle is still `new Set([400,401,403,404,409,422])`, and a probe written to that wording concludes the fix is missing. What shipped is an escape hatch inside `handleFailure` (`const isConflict = 409 === e.status; return 'permanent' !== cls || isConflict ? …retry… : markFailed(…)`), which deliberately leaves the classification alone so 409 still maps to `sync.failure.conflict` ("Termin bereits dokumentiert"). The persisted entry shows the divergence directly: `state: "pending"` with `lastError.class: "permanent"`. **Second thing to hold onto: the sync bar and the notice are SUPPOSED to disagree.** `counts()` gained `awaitingSend = pending+syncing+retrying` beside `unsynced = …+failed`; the bar reads `unsynced`, `useRecoveredWorkNotice` reads `awaitingSend` and fires only above zero. Seeded 2 pending + 1 filed, one screen reads "3 Änderungen nicht gesendet" AND "(2)" — a test expecting them to agree reports a defect that is not there. **Other traps:** `hydrate()` runs `purgeExpired()` (30 days by `createdAt`) and rewrites `syncing` → `pending`, so seeds must be stamped `Date.now()`; the notice is a **toast** fired once per engine mount behind a `useRef` latch, so it must be polled from the first paint; `innerText` splits the bar into "Stand von vor 0 Min" and "· N Änderungen nicht gesendet" as separate lines, which defeats `TherapistBoardV2Page.syncStatus()`'s single-line match; and AC3's "the bar started out showing work" is **not reliably samplable** — with the server answering the queue can drain inside one 250ms poll, so that precondition is taken from the notice count and the seed instead. **Run at `--workers=1`** — each test mints a session with `POST /auth`, which #3462 throttles at 5/min per username; in parallel every test fails at login for a reason unrelated to this ticket. **Observation for the PM** (copy predates #3594, so not an AC failure): during an outage the Sync-Status panel's only line is "Alle Änderungen synchronisiert" while the bar above says 2 unsent — rare before, because a stuck entry was filed quickly and the panel then had something to show, but now the normal outage state by design. Also "1 Änderungen nicht gesendet" gets the German plural wrong at N=1, and the ticket's own Localization Reference carries the same string. Uses `Pages/therapist/therapist.sync-queue.page.ts` |

| `sa_pkv_invoice_fee_lines.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PkvInvoiceFeeLines @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.12 (#3533) a PKV invoice's printed line-item table must sum to its own printed total, with every fee counted in the total shown as its own line. **The fix IS deployed on staging, and the before/after pair proves it:** two VOs of the same shape (`KG-H`+`HBH-PT`+ an **unmaterialised `AB-P`**) — **R426-36** (inv 296) rendered **13.07.2026** prints two lines and NO AB-P line, while **R426-93** (inv 629) rendered **28.08.2026** prints the same two plus `1 x Verwaltungsaufwand Arztbericht`. **The mechanism:** carrying a one-time fee is not what puts a VO at risk — the fee must also be **unmaterialised**, i.e. have no `ActivityTreatment` row. Matched pair, both carrying exactly `MLD45H`+`HBH-PT`+`AB-L`: presc **23602/R526-55** has rows `{MLD45H:6,HBH-PT:6,AB-L:1}` and prints `1 x AB-L 9,30`; presc **27764/126-5** has `{MLD45H:10,HBH-PT:10}` and **no AB-L row**, so the calculator still charges the 9,30 (a one-time fee is attributed to the VO's first-ever activity) while the pre-fix renderer, walking `activity->getActivityTreatments()`, could not print it → **953,10 against a printed 962,40**. Predicate `one_time_fee ∧ ¬materialised` is predictive: of **132** PKV invoices, 11 carry a one-time fee, **3** have it unmaterialised, and exactly the **2** with a non-zero tariff show the gap (126-5, 426-15) — the ticket's own count. **Only `AB-L` (9,30) and `L-ED` (150,00) carry a non-zero PRIVAT tariff**; the other seven one-time fees are 0,00, so they can vanish from a table without moving the total — which is why the deployment pair demonstrates the LINE APPEARING (AC1's literal wording) rather than a gap closing, and why L-ED is the 150,00 EUR ceiling on the worst silent gap. **Compare table sum against the PRINTED total, never `totalRevenue`**: `getInvoiceAmount()` prefers the issue-time snapshot (#3093), so R426-70/R526-59 are CONSISTENT documents 78,00 and 212,60 below their VO's live revenue and would be false positives. **Traps:** the invoice prints a fee by DESCRIPTION, never by code — matching on the code only works for `AB-L` (whose description IS "AB-L"), while `AB-P`/`AB-E` both print as "Verwaltungsaufwand Arztbericht"; `GET /activities?prescription.id=` is silently ignored (use `?prescription=`); regenerating an issued invoice answers **400 "A cancellation reason is required to regenerate an issued invoice."**; and `parseInvoice` retries once because a torn download parses to a null total, which `isInternallyConsistent` would otherwise report as a huge gap. **Non-discriminating fixtures pinned by a test:** R426-92 (VO 8091-2) carries no one-time fee, and R526-69 (VO 8922-1) has its AB-E materialised AND worth 0,00 — both sum correctly on either build, so neither is evidence. **Findings** → `fixme`: R426-36 keeps its pre-fix document (AC4) and its zero-tariff fee hides the real exposure (3 VOs structurally affected, not 2 — and #3378 could backdate a tariff onto it); R426-57/R526-18/126-12 print a table **LARGER** than their total (+41,80/+41,80/+24,00), the mirror image no AC describes; 226-2/326-43 are short by whole **treatment** lines (78,00 and 312,00, the two largest gaps) on VOs with no fees at all. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.pkv-invoice-lines.page.ts` |
| `sa_invoice_stored_only.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @InvoicePdfs @StoredOnly @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.12 (#3495) every invoice download serves the STORED file or fails — the `?? $renderer->renderPdf()` fallback is gone from all four paths. **Deployed on staging and both new branches observed live** (2026-08-31): `GET /status` reports `3.12.0`, the bundle carries the `409===res.status` branch plus "Diese Rechnung ist derzeit nicht verfügbar…" (the German string is frontend-only — `api/` has no translator), and the missing-file state was **manufactured**, since it cannot be found: all **550** invoices and all **10** Stornos serve stored files. The one client-reachable way is a NEWLY created invoice — `POST /prescriptions/{id}/generate-invoice` on a copayment-eligible VO with none writes the PDF from an async worker, so `pdf_path` is NULL for ~30 s. VO 8869-2 → invoice 630 (R726-24): **409 `"No stored PDF exists for this invoice."` at +2.6 s, stored at +28 s**; VO 3277-4 → 631 (R126-120), and a bulk of `[630 stored, 631 missing]` returned `Zuzahlung_Wieland_R726-24.pdf` + **`Fehlerbericht.txt`** ("Diese Rechnungen konnten nicht erstellt werden: R126-120"), the same batch holding both PDFs 26 s later (AC3). **Regenerating an existing draft opens no window** — the old stored file is served until the new one lands (~43 s) — so the two mutating tests are **env-gated** (`INVOICE_409_PRESCRIPTION_ID=<id>`): creating an invoice is irreversible, and the only copayment-eligible uninvoiced VO left is **6314-2, which is #3426's exclusion fixture and must never be invoiced**. **Traps:** the sweep uses `HEAD` (6x cheaper, and its `content-disposition` still names the stored file); nonexistent ids and a cancelled invoice under `type: copayment` are dropped from a bulk zip **silently**, never reported; and the statuses are deliberately distinct — **409** = stored file missing, **404 "This invoice has no Stornorechnung."** = no Storno. AC4's missing-Storno branch → `fixme` (a fresh Storno needs CANCELLING an invoice) and AC5's PKV branch → `fixme` (no client-reachable PKV creation). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.invoice-pdfs.page.ts` |
| `sa_invoice_export.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @InvoiceExport @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3493) the **Rechnungsexport** screen at `/invoice-export`: one Gesellschaft + a date range → an on-screen table and a CSV of every invoice issued in that window. **Deployed; all 8 ACs verified live.** Three surfaces are compared directly — the FILE (`POST /invoices/audit/export` with **`{entity, from, to}`** — note `entity`, not `billingEntity`, and `from`/`to`, not the list's `issuedFrom`/`issuedTo`), the LIST behind the table (`GET /invoices?billingEntity=&issuedFrom=&issuedTo=&groups[]=invoice-list:read`, the screen's own request), and the SCREEN. **The headline evidence is the partition**: the seven per-Gesellschaft exports hold **all 550 invoices, each under exactly one Gesellschaft, 0 missing and 0 duplicated** — for a tax export "nothing was silently dropped" is the property that matters, and file-vs-list row sets match exactly for all 7. AC8 is proven on real data: all **10** cancelled invoices carry their Storno number and each Storno is its own **negative** row — and they are only in the file because the filter is `COALESCE(issueDate, createdAt)`: **every Storno document on staging has a NULL `issueDate`**, so an issueDate-only filter would drop all 10. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.invoice-export.page.ts` |
| `sa_auth_token_security.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @AuthTokenSecurity @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 #3460 (SEC-01.4 transparent refresh + TTL cut) and #3463 (SEC-04 token material out of plaintext storage), **web target**. **Both deployed** — #3460 as `857d30e19`/`4de74fd4a`/`ca9152701` (PR #3542), #3463 as `4d6feab18`. **AC1 measured from the token, not the yaml**: `token_ttl` reads `%env(int:JWT_TOKEN_TTL)%` in every block, so the grep the AC describes can only ever show a variable — staging's issued JWT has `exp - iat` = **3600 s on both the login and the refresh path**. Rotation verified as a CHAIN (4 consecutive refreshes, 5 distinct tokens) rather than one hop, a spent token is refused 401, and **replaying a spent token revokes the whole family** — the live descendant, checked working immediately before, dies too. That property is worth proving because #3463's accepted-risk note leans on it. #3463 AC3 verified the way the AC demands, by INSPECTING the stored value: `auth-state` holds only `{user, fetching}` with no JWT anywhere in the payload, `auth-refresh-token` holds the opaque refresh token, `_auth_token_migration_done` is set — matching `packages/auth/README.md`'s documented web strategy (access token in memory, refresh token in localStorage as an accepted risk reviewed by 2026-11-30). The web client therefore calls `/token/refresh` exactly **once on boot**, which is asserted. **Traps:** replaying a spent token destroys the session you are holding, so tests must log in fresh per case and never share a token chain (this is also why `.auth/*.json` is single-use); and a boot refresh is not evidence of the 401 path — count refreshes inside a window. **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) **#3460 AC6 is NOT verified here** — the ticket calls 5+ concurrent 401s collapsing into one refresh "the highest-risk regression in the whole epic", and the best in-app burst reachable from a browser put only 1–2 requests in flight, so the test **skips with the count** rather than passing on weak evidence (1 refresh for 2 concurrent 401s was observed, which is consistent but not a demonstration); (b) the README's accepted-risk note leads with "the access-token TTL is 1h" as what bounds the web exposure, but the refresh token beside it in localStorage mints a fresh access token on demand — this suite's own `mintUiSession()` is the working demonstration — so what actually bounds it is rotation + family reuse detection, both verified above; (c) #3463's AC1/AC3-native/AC4/AC5/AC9 and #3460's Android half of AC5 need a device, with the repo-side evidence recorded inline (`expo-secure-store ~15.0.8`, the 2048-byte SecureStore size guard, the OTA-runtime fingerprint note). Also covers **#3516** (`useRoles` loading never resolved with no user, fixed in `cefde109d`/PR #3605): a hook has no endpoint, so the deployed bundle is the surface — the minified effect body reads `n(e.user?.roles||[]),l(!1)`, i.e. the loading setter runs **unconditionally**, with no `e.user&&` guard. **Finding** → `fixme`: the bug had **no user-visible symptom on web** — its one consumer that branches on `loading` (`app/(tabs)/admin/messenger.tsx`, which renders `null` until it resolves) is unreachable signed out, because the app's auth guard redirects every route to the login form first (measured: `/admin/messenger`, `/therapist`, `/dashboard` all land on `/` with the login form). Real bug, correctly fixed, lower severity than "spins indefinitely" implies. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.auth-refresh.page.ts` |
| `sa_optica_export_problems.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @OpticaExportProblems @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3343) the six Optica export checks now surface BEFORE the .rz download — an amber badge on the GKV-Abrechnung batch list, a warning marker with an inline issue panel inside a batch, and a non-blocking warning when a batch is marked Ready to Send. **Deployed (PR #3414); AC3–AC9 verified live.** All three surfaces read one endpoint, **`GET /billing_batches/export-problems`**, which returns one item per UNSENT batch with a failing VO carrying `OpticaExportValidator`'s groups verbatim plus `doctorId`/`practiceId` for the fix links — **sent batches are excluded server-side**, so AC4 is a property of the data, not a UI filter, and both halves are asserted. Live: **8 unsent batches, all with problems, 38 problem VOs**, against 45 sent with no badge; the badge SET is compared to the endpoint id-for-id (63–70) and each badge's text to `problemVoCount` ("Export-Probleme · 13 VOs"), so a badge on the wrong row cannot pass. AC5/AC6/AC9 on batch 63: the badge opens the batch, every marker belongs to a flagged VO, and of its **9 clean VOs the 5 painted on the page carry no marker at all** (the cell renders `null`, so absence is the assertion). Verified on screen as Super Admin: **99651-1 paints "Praxis" and 7381-4 paints "ER bestellt selbst"** — the ticket's own repro VOs, both re-read from the API first so a fixture whose status changed cannot report the board as broken — each with **no button, `cursor: auto`, no `aria-label`** (AC2: a read-only `StatusPill`, so there is nothing a later edit could re-enable), while `375-46`/`532-13` keep `button[aria-label="Status: Vom Admin"|"Status: Von Therapeut"]` with `cursor: pointer`. AC3: the editor opens and offers **exactly `["Vom Admin","Von Therapeut"]`** — #2614's rule, still holding, and the reason the display had to be fixed somewhere other than the options array — closed with Escape, nothing selected, and the stored value re-read afterwards. **AC4 needs a plain `ROLE_ADMIN` and that is where the run first lied:** `mintUiSession` replaces only the refresh token, and the project's saved `storageState` still carries the **Super Admin's** `{user:{roles}}`, which the app reads while `/me` is in flight — so the board rendered `isSuper`, every cell was editable and AC4 read as failing. `openBoardWithColumn()` now clears `auth-state` in an init script and the test **asserts the roles it is actually running with** before reading the lock; as a genuine admin, `5104-1` (By Admin + validated + Aktiv) is `aria-disabled="true"` with `cursor: default` and a **forced** click opens nothing, while the same admin gets a working editor on the unvalidated `375-46` and a read-only pill on `99651-1`. Status, VO Status and Folge-VO Status use the same pattern, and a first probe grabbed `Status: Aktiv` from Beh. Status and concluded the editor showed the wrong value; every read here is scoped to the header's x-band. **The column sits at x≈2017 on a 1440px viewport**, inside the table's horizontally-scrolling port — reading works at any scroll offset but a click by coordinates silently does nothing, so go through a locator, which scrolls it in. **The editor is not a `role="menu"`** — it paints two plain leaves stacked under the cell, so it is found by position and must be POLLED for (reading straight after the press returns `[]`, which looks exactly like "the editor did not open"). **A locked cell must be clicked with `force`** — an ordinary click waits out its actionability timeout instead of answering the question, and a forced click is the stronger statement anyway. **The sort is a server round trip**, so the header click is wrapped in `settleAfter` — a fixed sleep returns the PREVIOUS ordering and reads as the sort doing nothing. **The column's rows are separated by zero-width leaves** that survive `trim()` and are truthy, so a bare `filter(Boolean)` keeps them and they read back as `""` strays. **Never `page.reload()` after `mintUiSession`** (single-use refresh token, #3460) — which is why the on-screen sort test does no search, the board's search box being reload-only to reset. **Cross-ticket:** the root cause is #3759's open hygiene ticket — `FollowupOrderingStatus` stores display strings as backing values, which is what made a lexicographic sort look plausible; the filter is keyed off `FollowupOrderingStatus::*->value` so it carries a rename automatically. **Read-only** — no ordering status is written; the one editor opened is dismissed with Escape. | `sa_praxis_appointment_reminder.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PraxisReminder @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.14 (#3640, commit `d278cbca5` / PR #3707, plus **`therapios/praxis#8`**) every practice appointment gets one reminder text at the practice's own lead time, with a cancel link. **Deployed on both halves; 10 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **This is the suite's first Praxis Flow ticket and the thing to know is that Praxis holds NO DATA OF ITS OWN.** `therapios/praxis` is a second front end over the same API — appointments, rooms, SMS consent, the message log and one-time links are all Doctrine entities in `api/` — so the whole of this ticket's state is readable from `api.staging.therapios.de` with an ordinary Flow token, and only AC4 needs the Praxis app itself (deployed separately at **`https://praxis-staging.curano.de`**, found in that repo's `deploy.yml`; the `.env.example`'s `staging-api.therapios.de` does not resolve). **Deployment is decided twice and neither time by `/status`:** the API half by the new serialized column `reminderLeadHours` (present on all 259 facilities), the Praxis half by the served `/login` chunks. **The Praxis login page ships BOTH translation dictionaries**, which is what makes AC4 verifiable with no Praxis credentials — all seven German/English pairs plus both sample reminder texts are asserted off the deployed build (#3337's technique on a different app). **The scheduler is STATELESS and that IS AC2:** nothing is scheduled at booking time, each hourly pass asks what is due *under the setting as it stands*, so AC2 is asserted structurally — **no `remind*`/`dueAt`/`scheduledFor` key exists anywhere on an appointment** — and arithmetically, by re-deriving the due set at 24 h and at 48 h and requiring the two to differ, plus a live `24 → 48 → 24` round trip on the test practice that leaves every already-sent reminder byte-identical. The pass runs on `cron(0 6-19 * * ? *)` and **its fingerprint is in the log**: a scheduler reminder is stamped at :01 past an hour, which is how one it sent is told from one a QA sent by hand. Verified: AC1's rule ported exactly (`startsAt <= now || remindAt > now` → not due; `bookedAt > remindAt` → booked inside the lead time; note the boundaries — starting exactly now is NOT due, booked exactly AT remind-at IS eligible) and both directions checked — **every reminder in the log was inside its appointment's due window when sent**, and the **catch-up invariant** (an appointment may only still be un-reminded if it entered its window after the last pass) holds with 0 misses; at most one reminder per appointment; the 08:00–20:00 Berlin window (sends measured at 08:01 and 16:01 Berlin, and the window is #3637's rule, not re-implemented); `Assert\Choice` refusing 36/0/12 with **422** and null with **400**, value unrestored-because-unwritten. **The fixtures are chosen so an absence has only ONE explanation**, which on this ticket is the whole difficulty — a patient without SMS consent produces no reminder whatever the rule does: AC1's "booked inside the lead time" row is demonstrated on appointments **16/17/20, all with consent**, and "a cancelled appointment is skipped" on **appointment 6**, which sat cancelled through its entire due window with a consented patient. **TRAPS:** **`/patients?smsConsentGranted=` is accepted and SILENTLY IGNORED** — `true`, `false` and unfiltered each answer all 8,401 rows — so consent can only be read per patient, and getting it wrong inverts the result (pinned by an assertion); **a blocked reminder writes NO history row by design** (`remind()` returns early on `blocked_no_consent`, because "reminder sent" against somebody never texted is the sentence a receptionist would read out), so the message log and the history are deliberately NOT the same set and a test requiring both on every message fails on a correct build; **`dropped_staging` is not blocked** and is the only status on staging that can write a history row; **de-duplication reads the MESSAGE LOG**, so any reminder row from any source suppresses the scheduled one forever; and **`date` + `startTime` are two columns meaning Berlin wall clock** (`2026-09-22T00:00:00+00:00` + `1970-01-01T08:20:00+00:00`), so reading either as UTC moves every appointment by the offset. **FINDING 1 — the default lead time is 24, the AC now says 48.** Management decided 48 on **16 Sep**; PR #3707 merged **15 Sep** and ships `DEFAULT_REMINDER_LEAD_HOURS = 24`, migration `Version20260915114322` writing 24 onto every row, and praxis#8's zod `REMINDER_LEAD_HOURS_DEFAULT = 24`. Measured live: **all 259 facilities read 24 h, none reads 48**. The PM's comment says "no action needed … unless the default lives somewhere that needs a separate change" — it lives in three places. **FINDING 2 — RESOLVED 2026-09-25, and the armed prediction is what caught it.** When written, AC1's history half had **ZERO `reminder_sent` rows across all 37 appointments**, and every candidate is accounted for, so it is a data gap rather than a defect: the two scheduler reminders were correctly blocked for consent, and the one consented appointment inside its window (appt 3) had its scheduled reminder suppressed by a hand-run #3637 probe already sitting in the log — which is also the concrete cost of log-based de-duplication. **The live prediction that was left armed has FIRED:** the logging test now reads **1 `reminder_sent` row — appointment 40, stamped 2026-09-21T12:01:52Z**, carrying `textMessage` and resolving in the message log exactly as the armed assertion required. The log now holds 7 reminders (2 not blocked, 5 `blocked_no_consent`) across 72 appointments, so the deliberate asymmetry the file documents — a blocked reminder writes NO history row — is now observable on real data rather than argued from code. AC3 → `fixme` with three measured walls: **`cancelled_via_link` occurs zero times** in the whole appointment history (all 6 cancellations are staff ones), the one-time link is only ever delivered in a text body and every staging send is dropped or blocked (`/patient_links` is 404 by design), and redeeming one is irreversible — PR #3707 ships no code for it ("AC3 needed no code at all"), it is #3638's redeemer covered by `PatientLinkPublicTest`. **Also for the PM:** the Testing Guidance says to use "a Practice Manager account for Praxis Rheinstraße", but **Praxis Rheinstraße (facility 104) has no `praxis_flow_access` row at all** — the three practices that do are 253, 254 and 206 — so that QA route cannot be followed as written. **Read-only apart from the AC2 round trip**, which moves facility 253 (`Praxis Flow Test Praxis (PM)`) and restores it in a `finally`. Uses `Pages/praxis/praxis.appointment-reminder.page.ts` |
| `sa_flow_boards_session_state.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @FlowBoardsSessionState @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3761, commit `dc0b292c6` on `release/3.14.0`, a `Ref` trailer with **no PR** — the same commit also ships #3762's Aktualisieren button) reloading Flow Boards in the same browser tab must reopen the board, the Gesellschaft and that board's filters, while a new tab starts on the defaults. **Deployed; 6 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **The whole feature is three sessionStorage keys**, which makes it both observable and seedable: `FlowBoardsScreen` swapped three `useState` calls for `useSessionState` (the `CRMScreen` pattern) — `flowBoards.selectedTab`, `flowBoards.selectedEntity` (the whole `EntityOption`, not its id) and `flowBoards.filtersByTab`. That helper is the reusable part of this file. Verified: **AC1/AC2** on the ticket's own recipe — Admin-Performance + Monat + `Curano Hamburg GmbH`, reloaded, with all three restored **in storage AND on screen** (level re-read through `activeLevel()`, the company off the header, and the board identified by its own content); **AC2's isolation** with two boards holding different levels at once (Management `tag`, Therapeuten-Orga `monat`, the untouched Admin-Performance still `woche`) surviving a reload and still separate when switched back — different values on two boards simultaneously is the only shape that shows the slices are separate rather than merely persisted; **AC3** in a fresh browser context, which IS a new tab (its own sessionStorage) — `selectedTab` and `selectedEntity` both null, **Woche**, **Alle Gesellschaften**, Management painted; **AC4** by seeding an unknown board key before the first navigation — it renders Management with **no error surface at all**; and a robustness case the commit's own unit test covers, here live: **corrupt JSON plus a partial slice** (`'{not json'` and a slice carrying only `level`) still opens the default board at the stored level, because `useSessionState` falls back on any parse throw and the context spreads the defaults UNDER the stored slice so a field added since is never undefined. **TRAPS:** **`selectGesellschaft()` opens the picker itself** — calling `openGesellschaft()` first leaves the list covering the trigger and the second open HANGS to the test timeout, since `actionTimeout` is 0 project-wide (that is exactly how the first probe here died at 300 s); **a board tab is NOT an `isSegmentActive()` segment** — all five report false whichever is open, so the active board is decided by a string only that board paints (`Privatpatient:innen` for Management, `Ø / Woche (letzte 4 Wo.)` for Admin-Performance) with the other's marker asserted ABSENT, so a half-rendered board cannot pass; and **`activeTab` is DERIVED and never written back**, so after AC4's fallback the stored value is still the unavailable board — AC4 has to be read off the screen, not out of storage. **AC4's permission variant has no fixture and the file says so:** every Flow-Boards account on staging is a Super Admin seeing all five tabs (an Admin gets no board at all, #3173), so an unknown key is used, which takes the same `visibleTabs.some(...)` branch. **Read-only on the server** — every request the board makes is a GET; | `sa_to_risk_grouping.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @RiskGrouping @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3774, commit `347274e1e`, a `Ref` trailer with **no PR**) the Therapeuten-Orga risk table groups by therapist for the billing-backlog view and gains its own CSV export; the Abrechnungs-Stau banner is retired from both boards and the Management board carries a plain summary line instead. **Deployed on both halves; 10 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **Deployment is decided twice and `/status` answers for neither** (release not commit — #3704; silent about the frontend — #3705): the API half by its two BRAND-NEW routes answering at all against a **404 control** (`GET /kpis/management/unbilled-summary` 200, `POST /kpis/orga/risks/export` 200, both `zzz-not-a-route` controls 404), the frontend half by what the board paints. **The oracle is the board's OWN payload, never the screen:** `GET /kpis/orga/risks` serves all **5,501 rows** carrying `tile`/`therapistName`/`teamName`/`revenue`, so `riskGroups.ts`'s ordering rule is ported in the page object and the screen compared against it — which is the only way AC3 is checkable at all, since the sort only shows itself across ~130 groups. It excludes every `duplikatOffen` ROW but not a VO that is also stale (9 Duplikat ids → 7 exported, exactly the 7 that carry a non-Duplikat row; a VO whose only row is Duplikat is never written). CSV header verified byte for byte, `;`-delimited with a UTF-8 BOM. **FINDING — AC5's two clauses conflict in the view the board OPENS in, and the PM's own QA step fails there.** With no tile selected the groups paint more rows than the CSV holds, because **some VOs appear under more than one tile** and the export deliberately writes each VO once. AC5 asks for "every row currently grouped … one row per VO, in the same order as the on-screen groups" — those cannot both hold, and the commit chose one-row-per-VO (the sane reading); but the Testing Guidance's "confirm the row count and therapist names match the on-screen groups" cannot be followed in the default state. **With the Fertig tile selected the two agree exactly**, so only the no-tile case diverges. **EVERY FIGURE ON THIS ROW DRIFTS DAILY AND IS DATED, NOT PINNED** — the Fertig tile read 1,179 on 2026-09-23 midday, **1,625** that evening once #3775 widened the population, and **1,649** on 09-25; the no-tile split went 5,492/4,167 → **5,949/4,596** (difference 1,325 → 1,353) and the groups 133 → **136** over the same two days. The file survives all of it because every assertion is an INVARIANT over one payload (the CSV equals the groups; the summary equals the tile to the cent) rather than a count — which is the whole reason to write it that way, and why the one test title that did hardcode "1,325" was corrected. AC7 verified twice: `unbilled-summary` equals the Fertig tile and the sum of its rows to the cent (two providers, one figure — not a tautology), and the Management board paints **"1.649 fertig behandelte VOs noch nicht in der Abrechnung · ≈ 931.859 € direkt abrechenbar"** (2026-09-25) with no chevron and no button role. AC6 on both boards. AC1's flat half on all three other tiles (0 groups, no export button) and on the **Admin-Performance board**, which shares the `RiskWorklist` component and is the realistic leak. **Traps:** `tiles.duplikatOffen` reports **8** while **9 rows** carry that tile (pre-existing; #3775 owns tile population), so the tile counts are not a safe row oracle; the group header and its subtotal are **separate leaves** (`"{count} VOs · {amount}"`), so a locator expecting both together finds nothing; the Heilmittel header reads **"HM"**, not the Localization Reference's "Heilmittel" (only the CSV uses the full word); **the board opens with NO tile selected**, which is itself one of AC1's two grouped cases; and the boards append **" (Inaktiv)"** to a deactivated therapist (#3210) while the payload carries the bare name — 8 groups, and a raw name key silently skips exactly those (a first pass reported "125 of 133 matched" without saying why). **A readiness lesson this file learned on itself:** the first `waitForRiskTable()` accepted "headers OR groups", the headers paint first, and `paintedGroups()` then returned `[]` — which reads exactly like the table not being grouped. It now takes `expectGroups`, waiting for the thing the caller is about to read; the flat tiles pass `false`, because zero groups is the assertion there and can never be waited for. **The same mistake was found and fixed in #3575's dot spec**, which waits on a therapist NAME and then reads DOTS — two different payloads (`working-hours` vs `efficiency-buckets`) — and failed with 0 dots on a build where a probe measured 102 moments later; `waitForDots()` added. **Fixed 2026-09-25:** the AC2/AC5 localization test asserted `flowBoards.duplikatVo` = "VO-Nr.", and that key is **gone from the deployed dictionary** — #3774 moved both boards onto the one shared `RiskWorklist`, so the VO column now renders from the shared `drilldownVoNumber` ("VO #"), which is why #3774's `GROUP_COLUMNS` records the header as "VO #". The test now asserts the retirement AND the shared key as a pair, so a future re-introduction of a duplikat-specific key is visible rather than silently giving one column two keys — the drift #3711 flagged. AC2's collapse/expand → `fixme`: the group header carries no testid, no `role="button"` and no `aria-expanded` (the RNW gap #3400/#3343/#3505 each recorded on this board family), and this is the slowest section on staging, so a click landing mid-repaint is swallowed and reads as "the toggle is broken" only under load; covered upstream by the commit's 409-line `riskGroupedView3774.test.tsx`. **`sa_flow_boards_billing_backlog.spec.ts` (#3180) is retired by this ticket** and was rewritten rather than deleted: its four banner tests are `fixme`'d (the banner is hidden, not removed, pending #3775) and a single green test now ASSERTS the banner's absence on both boards — because their old gate was `test.skip(banner.count() === 0, 'No billing backlog in the current filter scope')`, so a removed feature and a quiet data day produced the identical silent pass. **Run this file at `--workers=1` and expect to re-run it:** these are the slowest reads on staging and `unbilled-summary` answered **504** once mid-session while the route was demonstrably deployed — which failed the DEPLOYMENT gate and, in a `serial` describe, cascaded every remaining test to "did not run". A 5xx is the server being unhealthy, not the route being absent; only **404** means absent, which is what the 404 controls beside it are for. `status()`, `risks()` and `unbilledSummary()` all retry a 5xx with a widening pause rather than believing it. **THREE PINS IN THIS FILE WENT STALE ON 2026-09-23 when #3785/#3775 shipped in PR #3788 — all fixed here, none a defect.** `GROUP_COLUMNS` 8 → **10** (`Ausst. Datum` 3rd, `Beh. Status` 9th) and `CSV_HEADER` 8 → **11** (+ `Thema`): this ticket's AC4/AC5 govern the order of the columns THEY name, so those are now asserted as a **subsequence** with the full current set checked beside them (`ORIGINAL_GROUP_COLUMNS` / `ORIGINAL_CSV_HEADER`). And `flowBoards.abrechnungsStau` is **gone from the dictionary and the bundle entirely** (0 occurrences) — the planned sequel, since this ticket's Out of Scope deferred the deletion ("hidden, not removed… before that code is deleted") and #3788 is titled "…and retire the Abrechnungs-Stau code"; the two strings AC7 actually depends on (`backlogBannerText`, `backlogDirektAbrechenbar`) survive, and the assertion now pins the retirement instead. **The lesson, learned the slow way: after a ticket widens a table, grep the whole spec for sibling pins and RE-RUN — fixing the one assertion that failed is not the same as fixing the file** (three separate runs were needed to find all three). **Read-only** — every request a GET except the export POST, which writes nothing. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.to-risk-grouping.page.ts` |

AC5 is scoped *"for any one therapist"*, and **per therapist the sets agree exactly, 0 in either direction**. (b) **PKV must be excluded first and AC5 says so** — AC6's last row is the reason: a PKV VO with an active invoice counts on the Orga surfaces and NOT on Admin-Performance, **9 live instances, all `Fertig Behandelt`, all `isPrivate`**; comparing with PKV in yields nine spurious disagreements that are the specified behaviour. **A THIRD TRAP, and it is a field that does not exist where you look for it:** the signed-treatment gate must be read from a DIFFERENT field per surface. `activityCount` and `totalTreatments` are #3774's grouped-view columns — populated on every Orga row and **null on all 2,266 Admin-Performance rows** — so one shared check scores that surface as "none of them has a signed treatment" and reports AC1 failing on a correct build (it did here before being understood). Both surfaces DO carry `daysSince`, which is derived FROM the last signed treatment, so a VO without one could not have a value: that is the gate's observable consequence where the count is not exposed. Also verified: AC1's billing clause (`billingBatchCount` 0 across a 25-VO sample spanning all three statuses); AC2's differing age settings (Admin-Performance carries rows at/under 30 days, the Orga tile none); AC3's exception intact and NOT extended to the Orga surfaces; AC5's third surface — `completedUnbilledOver30Count` equals the risk tile therapist-for-therapist, and `completedUnbilledCount` equals the Admin-Performance set **plus** the PKV VOs rule 3 excludes (that figure is one number per therapist and cannot be narrowed by insurance type, so the comparison adds them back rather than pretending it can). **For the PM — the ticket's own Developer Reference query was wrong, twice, and the dev caught it in review** (`5d4e0ea51`): the supplied signed-treatment predicate was **not NULL-safe**, so legacy activities with unset rejection flags (**46,391** in the dev dump) read as "no signed treatment" and their VOs counted nowhere; and `DateInterval::days` is unsigned, so a **future-dated** treatment read as that many days OLD and was wrongly flagged "> 30 T". Both now go through one `ReadyForBillingVoSql::SIGNED_TREATMENT` constant shared with #3774's export and the Duplikat billing deadline, so "last treatment" is one date everywhere. Anyone implementing from that reference query would have shipped both defects. **Also observed here: #3724 shipped in the same window** — the rows now carry **`patientName`** with full names where they carried `patientInitials` on 2026-09-23, so `sa_flow_boards_patient_names.spec.ts`'s gated ACs should now run. AC4 (cache-version bumps, `orga_risks` → v6 etc.) → `fixme`: only falsifiable in the minutes AFTER a deploy, and any TTL had long expired by the time this file first read the endpoints; recorded that the first post-deploy read already returned the new population on all three surfaces. **Read-only.** Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.ready-for-billing-rule.page.ts` |

All four also have their decision recorded on the issue and their `authorization-matrix.md` rows updated. **MORE TRAPS:** **#3552's path is a URL SEGMENT** (`/document/download/{path}`, `requirements: ['path' => '.+']`) — a `?path=` probe answers 404 and reads like the route being gone, when the guard was never reached; **#3547 denies with 404, NOT 403**, deliberately, so a test expecting 403 calls a working gate broken; and **a 504 must never be scored as a denial** on these, the slowest endpoints on staging — `status()` retries a 5xx. **#3692** verified from the repo (needs an authenticated `gh`; the tests skip rather than fail without one, because an absent file and a 404 are indistinguishable): one shared `scripts/gates/secret-scan.sh` runs gitleaks `--staged --redact`, **exits 1 when gitleaks is absent (fails closed)**, is called as gate **1/6** by `api/bin/pre-commit` (first on purpose — the only gate there protecting something a later commit cannot undo) and by both `lint.yml` and `ci-selfhosted.yml`; `.gitleaksignore` records the 2026-09-19 one-off scan (**4 findings, all false positives, 0 live credentials**) with a justification comment above every fingerprint. **FINDING (#3736) — shipped as OPTIONAL, so the ticket's own Verify step does not hold.** The AC says "2FA is enforced for all admin roles" and Verify says "an admin account without a completed enrolment cannot reach an authenticated endpoint"; measured, a **`ROLE_SUPER_ADMIN` with `twoFactorEnabled: false` reaches `/prescriptions`, `/users` and `/patients` (200)**. Not a defect — the recorded product decision of 2026-09-19 was TOTP optional for everyone at launch, with enforcement as phase 2 of `docs/security/mfa-rollout.md`, **proposed 2026-11-16 and explicitly not committed**, needing a follow-up ticket that does not exist yet. The ACs and the rollout doc disagree and only the PM can close that. Deployment IS verified: all five 2FA routes answer **405** on GET (POST-only, registered) against a **404** control, and `twoFactorEnabled` is the only one of the five new user columns in a serialization group. | `sa_session_double_marker.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @SessionDoubleMarker @Security @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3711, commit `871ab6cc1` on `release/3.14.0`, a `Ref` trailer with **no PR**) every list of individual sessions must mark a **Doppelbehandlung** and show its doubled minutes. **Deployed on both halves; 8 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **Two of the five surfaces the ticket names did not exist and were built by the commit** — the Patient Management session table and the Berichte session export — both reading a brand-new admin-only **`GET /prescriptions/session-report`** (`TreatmentSessionRow` + `SessionReportProvider`), so that resource answering at all is the API half's deployment probe, which `/status` could not give (release, not commit — #3704). **The row is shaped so no client can get the arithmetic wrong, and the file asserts both halves of that contract:** `durationMinutes` is `Activity::getCalculatedTreatmentDuration()` **verbatim** — already doubled for a legacy (V1) Doppel VO, already NOT doubled for a V2 one, whose doubling lives in `ActivityTreatment::quantity` — while `doubleTreatment` is the **marker, not the arithmetic**, true for V1 and V2 alike so both render the same tag though their minutes legitimately differ. **AC1/AC2/AC3 are verified against the flag the therapist actually set**, session by session: for three mixed V1 VOs the report's marker equals `/activities`'s per-session `doubleTreatment` on every row, and — the assertion that gives "doubled" content — **the doubled rows are exactly twice the single rows of the SAME VO with the SAME Heilmittel** (3568-9 3×40 vs 2×20; 1281-16 2×40 vs 9×20; 1503-25 1×40 vs 10×20). AC4 is then checked at scale over a whole Berichte selection rather than on VOs chosen for having a double — **232 sessions, 70 double / 162 single, every row carrying a real boolean and a non-null duration**, with the window required to contain both kinds or the negative half would be vacuous. **AC2 is also verified ON SCREEN** on the one fixture where the contrast fits a single page: **patient 3019 holds exactly 13 sessions, all on VO 4056-1, twelve single at 30 Min and one double at 60 — and the double is the tenth by date**, so the painted table shows nine plain rows and one carrying `Doppelbehandlung ×2 · 60 Min` with `60 Min` in the Dauer column; a final test compares **all 10 painted rows against the API row for the same date, 0 mismatches**, which is the only thing that could silently drift between the new endpoint and the new screen. Access is asserted too: the resource and the operation both carry `ROLE_ADMIN` and a therapist gets **403 "Only Admins can read the session report."** (Article 9 health data, one row per session). **TRAPS:** **the tag is CSS-uppercased** — `DoubleTreatmentTag` sets `textTransform: 'uppercase'`, so `innerText` reads `DOPPELBEHANDLUNG ×2 · 60 MIN` while `textContent` keeps the real string (#3718), and both readings are asserted; **the `×` is U+00D7, not the letter x** — the ticket writes "Doppelbehandlung x2" and the shipped string is `Doppelbehandlung ×2` (#3666's `÷`); **the new keys are NOT where the Localization Reference implies** — they live at `common.double_treatment_x2*`, `patients.form.session*` and `reports.session_export.*`, and a first probe looking under `controls.*`/`patient_management.*` reported every one of them missing, so the dictionary is searched by VALUE (#3337); **`activity.doubleTreatment` is `true`, `false` or OMITTED on the same VO** (#3602), so only `=== true` is safe when building the expectation; **the endpoint FAILS CLOSED** — no filter, a partial Berichte selection and an unknown parameter all return **0 rows**, which is the opposite of `/prescriptions`, where an unregistered filter is ignored and the whole book comes back; and **the session table has no testids**, so rows are gathered by **vertical overlap with their date cell, never by a rounded y band** — the chip has its own padding and sits a few pixels off its row's baseline, so a band key files it as a row of its own and the test reports "no row is tagged" on a build where the tag is right there (that is exactly how this file first failed); the LAST row also needs an explicit floor of one row pitch, or it swallows the app's nav chrome and reports "▾ | Berichte" as its VO and therapist. **FINDING** — reported as a green test: the Testing Guidance says to **reuse** the existing tag component and translation key from the Flow Boards calendar, but the commit added a **second** key, `common.double_treatment_x2`, holding the identical German while `therapist_board.double_treatment_x2` is left in place and still used there — two keys, one string, two surfaces, free to drift. Defensible per the component's own docblock (the calendar's is a dashed BOX wrapping two ActivityCards, this one an inline chip) but a divergence from the ticket's guidance and a PM decision. **Also recorded:** the V2 "marker yes, ×2 no" branch is **unexercised on staging** — every reachable double VO is the legacy V1 shape, consistent with #3602's finding of 0 V2 double VOs in a 1,400-VO sample and its two named V2 fixtures not existing; and the commit deliberately leaves `Activity::getDuration()` returning the undoubled activity-level duration when one is set, with a measured blast radius of zero and its own ticket. **Read-only** — every request a GET; the screen half only navigates and reads, with ONE `goto` because `mintUiSession`'s refresh token is single-use (#3460). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.session-double-marker.page.ts` |
| `sa_reminder_letter_branding.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ReminderBranding @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3714, commit `a371c19f2`, PR #3726) a Zahlungserinnerung must print the entity's branding as it is TODAY, not the snapshot frozen on the invoice it chases; the invoice PDF keeps its own. **Deployed; AC1, AC2 and AC4 verified live. 6 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **The whole ticket is ONE COMPARISON and it needs no mutation:** the same invoice has two documents — the stored PDF (frozen) and the reminder (live) — and on staging every entity is already `isRebranded: true` while the older invoices froze Therapios, so the two must now DISAGREE. That disagreement is AC1, AC2 and AC4 in a single measurement. Nothing is written: a reminder is rendered on demand and never stored (#3559 — the controller renders and returns, no persist), and the invoice download serves the stored file (#3495). Measured on **4 of 4** eligible overdue PKV invoices: reminder `info@curano.de` / `www.curano.de` / banner / `“Hamburg, 20.09.2026”` against invoice `info@therapios.de` / `www.therapios.de` / no banner / `“16.06.2026”`. **FOUR independent markers per document, because `DocumentBrandingResolver::resolve()` sets each template variable separately** — a build that fixed the email but not the date line would pass a single-marker check. The one worth naming is `letter_date`: Curano renders **“City, DD.MM.YYYY”** and Therapios a bare **“DD.MM.YYYY”**, pure formatting and the easiest to miss. **AC4 is the control that stops AC1 being satisfied by “everything is Curano now”** — all 4 invoice PDFs still print Therapios on every marker. **Traps:** `pdfText()` drops a line of this letter (#3559) — use `pdfDocText()`; **a naive date-line regex swallows the preceding sentence** (“…zahlen Sie nichts. Hamburg, 20.09.2026” reads as a 28-character city), so `cityDateLine()` requires a capitalised word immediately before the comma; and **the branding snapshots are serialized in NO group** — not `billing:read`, not `invoice-list:read` — so “what did this invoice freeze?” is unanswerable from the API and is read from the invoice's own PDF instead. **FINDING** → reported as a green test: **only BRANDING went live.** `entity_name`, address, IBAN and BIC still come from `getIssuerNameSnapshot()`, deliberately, so the banking block keeps matching the invoice being chased — but the visible result is a reminder headed **“Therapios Hamburg 1 GmbH”** whose body carries the Curano banner and `info@curano.de`. Scope-compliant (“only the branding source”), yet AC1 reads “the letter shows Curano branding, not the Therapios branding the invoice was created with”, and a QA applying that literally will see the old company name at the top and file it. AC3 (banner currently OFF) → `fixme`: `rebrand_banner_enabled` resolves through `FeatureFlagService`, **Unleash is not provisioned in any deployed environment**, and the value arrives as the ECS env var `REBRAND_BANNER_ENABLED` — **staging ON, production OFF** by design — so producing that state is an ops change, not a test step; all five plausible client surfaces answer **404** (`/feature_flags`, `/settings`, `/system_settings`, `/configurations`, `/app_settings`), re-measured every run so the `fixme` cannot go stale. AC3's RENDERING half IS verified: the invoice documents pass `show_rebrand_banner: false` through the same resolver and template and print no banner. **Fixed 2026-09-25:** the deployment test asserted `isRebranded` over EVERY entity row and broke when #3775's load-test fixtures added two synthetic **`ZZPerf Entity …`** Gesellschaften carrying `isRebranded: false`. They issue no invoices, so they cannot weaken the comparison — but a global assertion cannot say that, and failed on a build where the ticket was still correct. The precondition is now scoped to the entities that actually ISSUE invoices, which is the property the comparison depends on; same class as #3775's own trap, where the ZZPerf rows make two tile totals look like a rule mismatch. **Read-only.** Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.reminder-branding.page.ts` |
| `sa_privat_basis_check_set.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PrivatBasisCheckSet @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3708, commit `28f402375`, migration `Version20260916070000`) Privat Basis VOs must be validated with the **GKV** check set instead of the PKV one #3608 gave them, minus eleven checks that do not apply to a Basistarif patient. **DEPLOYED 2026-09-17 and every AC verified — 9 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **The whole ticket is ONE SERVED COLUMN:** `GET /validations` exposes `applicableInsuranceTypes` per check (51 checks, one request), so AC1, AC3 and AC5 are all statements about that column — no sampled VO, no screen-scraping required. **The migration's rule is arithmetic, which makes the post-deploy target exact:** it appends `privat_basis` to every row carrying `public`, `WHERE description NOT IN (…11 names…)`. Measured before the deploy: **44 rows carry `public`, 15 `private`, 14 `privat_basis`, 6 disabled (empty list)**, and **all 14 Privat Basis rows are inside the 44** — a containment the spec asserts BEFORE using the number, because without it `public − excluded` is not a superset and the target silently under-counts. So after the migration Privat Basis must hold **exactly 44 − 11 = 33** — and the deployed build serves exactly that: **33 = 15 `vo_creation` + 17 `billing` + 1 `not_needed`**, up from 14 (6 + 8). **The verdict is reached three independent ways, before and after** — the registry, the create form's own endpoint (`preview-creation-validation`, transient, writes nothing: Privat Basis went 6 → **15** creation checks against GKV's 18), and the **Validierungskonfiguration screen** at `/validation-config`, whose Privat Basis column went 14 → **32 ✓**. `/status` reported `3.14.0` both before and after and could never settle it (#3704), which is exactly why all three probes are behavioural. **AC3 is verified as a MAPPING, which nothing else states:** the AC names five categories and the migration names eleven descriptions, so `EXCLUDED_BY_CATEGORY` pins the correspondence (copayment/exemption 3, 9-month 1, Blanko/LHB/BVB 4, discharge 2, group-switch 1 = 11) and each is asserted to exist, to be off for Privat Basis, **and to be a GKV check** — an "exclusion" GKV does not have either would be a migration no-op and a sign the list had drifted (0 such). **AC4 is a real assertion, not "a column is present":** the screen is display-only and rendered from the registry, so its tick count must equal the registry's — Privat Basis **14 ✓ == 14**, GKV Standard **40 ✓ == 40**. **THE TRAP in that comparison, and it reads as a 4-row product bug:** the GKV columns are split by **VO KIND** (Standard / LHB / BVB / Blanko), so "GKV Standard" is `public` AND `standard` — three checks are kind-specific (`blank_prescription_deadline`, `lhb_icd_in_list`, `bvb_icd_or_approval`) and correctly show ✗ there — and the screen has exactly two tables, so a `not_needed` check (`no_overlapping_prescriptions`) is rendered nowhere; 44 → 41 → 40. PKV, Privat Basis and UV/BG each get one column, so no kind filter applies to them. **Findings** → reported: (a) **AC2's severity half cannot fail, and saying so is the honest version of it** — `severity` is a property of the CHECK, one column per row with no per-insurance-type override (payload shape pinned by a test), so a check applying to both types necessarily carries the same severity and message. AC2's substance is verified instead by running **the same transient VO under both insurance types**: 15 shared checks, **0 verdict disagreements, 7 of them FAILING for Privat Basis** — which matters because a set where nothing failed would satisfy the equality trivially and prove nothing about AC2's wording ("a Privat Basis VO that FAILS a GKV-specific check"). The only creation checks GKV still runs and Privat Basis does not are ids 14/25/39, all AC3 exclusions. (b) **this ticket incidentally closed #3576's open finding** — `fee_area_matches_therapy_type` (id 54) was the one PKV creation check #3608 never gave `privat_basis`; it carries `public` and is not on the exclusion list, so the migration added it and **id 54 now applies to Privat Basis**; (c) the screen's **"Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-06-22"** predates both #3608 and this ticket, so a QA checking "did the screen update" by that stamp would conclude nothing changed. **Also for the PM:** AC5 calls PKV "the 11 shared checks" but the registry serves **15** — #3608's legacy wording; what AC5 actually requires is that the set does not CHANGE, which is structural, since the migration matches on `public` and never on `private`. **Traps:** `/validations` needs `itemsPerPage` raised or the default page hides most of the 51; six rows carry an EMPTY `applicableInsuranceTypes` (disabled) and must not read as "missing from Privat Basis"; the config screen has no per-cell testid, so columns are read by x-band and y-order. AC2's **billing** half → `fixme`: the 17 billing-timing checks have no preview endpoint, so demonstrating one failing needs a real Privat Basis VO in a failing state — cheap for a PM (4946-8 and 7551-9 are already past their start deadline with no treatment, which is what check 11 fires on), write-heavy for this suite. **Read-only.** Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.privat-basis-check-set.page.ts` |
| `sa_discharge_dates.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @DischargeDates @ReadOnly @Slow` | RC 3.14 (#3830, `4b1722ce7` api + `a394b0af8` app, both `Ref`-trailered with **no PR**) a **Entlassmanagement** VO must SHOW day 7 as its start deadline and day 12 as its valid-until date on every screen, and the existing expiring warnings must follow those dates. **Deployed on both halves; every AC verified. 10 passed, 0 `fixme`.** The display counterpart to #3800, which made the nightly check close the same VOs on the same two days; it supersedes two items of #3800's Out of Scope list. **Read-only** — every request a GET except the transient creation-validation preview, which writes nothing (#3576). **`/status` answers for neither half** (the release not the commit — #3704; silent about the frontend — #3705), so each is decided behaviourally: the API by the two SERIALIZED fields reading issue+7 / issue+12 against the non-discharge control **99680-1** at +28 (mandatory — both fields are legitimately null on many VOs, so "+7/+12" means nothing on its own), the app by the bundle carrying **`start_deadline_warning:['issueDate','insuranceType','isDischargeManagement']`** — the THIRD entry is the fix, and the array survives minification as an object literal of string keys. **AC8 is the rare AC that states its own before AND after, so it is checked against the ticket's own arithmetic:** 99699-1 reads **04.10.2026 / 09.10.2026** and 99700-1 **27.09.2026 / 02.10.2026**, both exactly as predicted, while the BG (99671-1, +14) and no-insurance-type (99672-1, +28) rows are untouched although both ARE marked Entlassmanagement — so the marker alone is not what moves the dates. **99700-1 is the sharper fixture:** its validity was 22.12.2026 under the ordinary Physio rule (three months from its first session), so the discharge branch overrides a rule that was producing a perfectly plausible value ~81 days later. **THE TEST-DESIGN MISTAKE, and it is the transferable lesson: AC2's four rows are NOT mutually exclusive.** They read *no session / first session by day 7 / first session after day 7 / VO closed*, and a single-label classifier that checks "closed" first files **12 of 14 fixtures under `closed`** and leaves row 3 empty — failing as "AC2 row 3 has no fixture" when 99667-1 is right there. The fix is two INDEPENDENT axes (a session state, plus `isClosed` as its own predicate), each required to occur. Whenever an AC table's rows can co-occur, one bucket per row is wrong. All four rows then have live fixtures, **including the boundary** — 99665-1's first session is ON day 7 and 99667-1's on day 8, and both show the same dates — and AC1's own note is asserted as a CONTRAST rather than a claim about one VO: a discharge VO with no session shows day 12 where the non-discharge **99683-1** with no session shows nothing at all, because the ordinary Physio validity only starts at the first treatment. **AC6 is driven read-only through the create form's own endpoint** (`POST /prescriptions/preview-creation-validation`, transient), which accepts `date` and `isDischargeManagement` — so the boundary is exercised exactly: 7 days back **passes**, 8 days back **fails** with the exact note *"Die Startfrist ist abgelaufen. Die VO kann automatisch verfallen."*, and the DISCRIMINATOR that makes it falsifiable is that at 8 days back a **non**-discharge VO still passes, same day and same insurance type, with the ordinary boundary intact (28 passes, 29 fails). **`/validations` is keyed by `description`, NOT `code`** — there is no `code` field, so a lookup by it silently finds nothing, every verdict reads `undefined` and the whole table prints as inapplicable (that is how this test first failed); `start_deadline_warning` is **id 47** (`vo_creation`) and `treatment_start_deadline` **id 11** (`billing`). **AC7 is verified end to end AND is caused by this ticket:** 99700-1 (valid 4 days out) IS on the Therapeuten-Orga `laeuftAb` tile and 99699-1 (11 days out) is not, and under the old rule 99700-1's validity was ~85 days out so it could not have appeared before — the warnings were deliberately unchanged and follow `validityDate` for free. AC3 as a truth table (GKV + Privat Basis get the new dates; PKV carries neither, excluded from expiry entirely per #3709; BG keeps +14; no type keeps +28; a VO not marked keeps +28).
