AC4 over the walked population — **28 in-scope discharge VOs, 26 physio + 2 ergo, and 8 with `urgentTreatmentNeed: true` all reading day 7**, which is AC4's hardest clause (the ordinary rule has its own 14-day urgent branch, so "day 7 wins" means the discharge branch is reached first) — plus **99673-1**, a Blanko discharge VO at +7/+12, Blanko being its own ordinary validity branch. **AC5 on both screens the ticket screenshots:** the VO form's *Wichtige Termine* panel reads `Behandlungsstartfrist = 27.09.2026, Gültigkeitsdatum = 02.10.2026`, and the T Board deadlines cell reads `04.10.2026 09.10.2026` for 99699-1 and **`Begonnen 02.10.2026`** for 99700-1 — which is also AC2's closing note (a screen that shows *Begonnen* in place of the start deadline keeps doing so), asserted rather than assumed, with the pre-fix `25.10.2026` / `22.12.2026` asserted ABSENT. **TWO TRAPS IN READING THE BOARD:** the VO number lives in the **frozen rail** (`v2-rail-cell-prescriptionId`) and **`v2-cell-prescriptionId` does not exist**, so a lookup there finds nothing and reads exactly like the fixture being absent from the board; and only ~12 rows of a 130-VO caseload are painted, so the fixture must be narrowed with the board's own SEARCH — never `page.reload()`, since `mintUiSession`'s refresh token is single-use (#3460). **The day meaning differs from the ordinary rules by one day, on purpose** (the ordinary rules expire ON the shown date, #3800's rule the day after), so day 7 and day 12 are the last valid days and must equal #3800's printed `Frist`/`Ende` — a spec that "aligns" the two off by one reports a defect that is not there. **AC9 → reported as a green FINDING rather than asserted, with the wall measured:** the billing check must keep the ordinary 28/14-day deadline until #3808 via `getOrdinaryTreatmentStartDeadline()`, which is deliberately **not serialized**; the only read-only surface is a stored verdict, the two discriminating VOs (**99667-1** first session day 8, **9489-1** day 11 — late under the discharge rule, fine under the ordinary one) carry **0** verdicts, and the five that do have one all started by day 7 so they pass under either rule. Producing one means `POST /prescriptions/{id}/check-billing-validation`, which **re-creates** the VO's billing verdict rows (measured on #3675's inert anonymized VO: the returned ids are new) — a write to live billing-validation state that can move a VO on or off the billing Validierung queue, so it is left as one call a PM can make. **Observations for the PM:** the app commit also fixes a bug **no AC mentions** — `VOsExpiringBanner` compared device-local midnight to a UTC instant, so a VO exactly five days out dropped off the banner east of UTC, i.e. in Germany; AC7 lists that window as "unchanged" and it was in fact wrong at the boundary. **No Logo discharge VO exists on staging**, so AC4's speech-therapy clause is unexercised; and 4 of AC5's 6 screens (T Board V1, billing validation, billing batch details, Praxis Flow patient file) plus 4 of AC7's 5 warnings were not driven — all read the same two serialized fields, which are verified, and that is stated rather than implied. **Runs in ~1 minute** by prefetching all 33 fixtures and their sessions in two batched requests (see the batching section above) and firing AC6's ten previews concurrently; the first version took 5.6 min. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.discharge-dates.page.ts` |
| `sa_gkv_insurer_required.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @GkvInsurerRequired @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.15 (#3810) an insurer (Versicherung) is required for GKV on both VO forms, the Create Patient pop-up and the patient form, and a GKV VO also needs its OWN Versichertenstatus. **Deployed — staging has moved to `3.15.0`; 6 passed, 1 skipped.** **THE RULE IS DELIBERATELY FORM-ONLY, and that decides how the whole ticket can be tested.** Its Out of Scope rules out "a rule on every save outside these forms … it would break Praxis Flow saves, the deceased marking, the merge command and the nightly jobs" — so there is **no server-side validation to probe**, AC9 holds by construction, and AC2/AC3/AC6/AC7 exist only on screen. **Which is also what makes it safe: every save this file attempts is one the rule must REFUSE, and a refused save writes nothing** — the fixtures are chosen so that even a build without the rule would write back the values the record already holds. Verified: **AC3** — VO **5921-2** (Aktiv, GKV, no own Versichertenstatus, patient 4677 has no insurer) refuses to save and shows **both** proposed messages verbatim, with the VO's status, its Versichertenstatus and the patient's insurer and legacy `insuranceCompany` all re-read afterwards and unchanged; **AC2's discriminator, which is what makes AC3 mean something** — VO **5806-7**, whose patient DOES have an insurer, fires the Versichertenstatus message and **NOT** the insurer one, so a build that gated the save on the wrong field or reused one message for both conditions fails here while passing AC3; **AC1** — the VO form paints `Versicherungsart`, `Versichertenstatus` and `Versicherung` with the last two **on the same row** (both y=1336.5, x=322.9 and 433.0), plus `Neue Versicherung erstellen`; **AC6** — the patient form marks the field **`Versicherung *`** on a GKV patient, with the search placeholder and the create link; **AC9** — the deceased dialog still answers for a patient the form would refuse (count 1, list 1). **THREE FILTERS THIS TICKET NEEDS ARE ALL SILENTLY IGNORED**, which is why its population cannot be selected server-side and why **#3812 exists to put it on a screen**: `/patients?insuranceType=` (#3710), `?exists[insuranceProvider]=false` AND `=true` (#3560) each return the whole **8,901**-row table. Worse, **`insuranceProvider` is OMITTED when null**, so on exactly the patients this ticket is about the key is absent from the item read and a presence check cannot tell "no insurer" from "wrong serialization group" — the two patients are compared instead (4677 absent / 4560 present). It is also absent from the LIST payload entirely, so the fixtures had to be found by reading patients one at a time. Sampling note: **50 of 50 sampled GKV VOs, and 25 of 25 Aktiv ones, carry no Versichertenstatus of their own** — the ticket's production figure is 3,301 of 8,352 open GKV VOs. **AC7's refusal is SKIPPED, with the reason measured rather than asserted:** the patient form's save control reads **`Änderungen speichern`** (not the VO form's `Speichern`) and carries `aria-disabled="true"` while the form is **pristine**, so the refusal is only reachable once a field has actually changed — and the insurer search box does not render until the field is opened, so it cannot be used to dirty the form harmlessly. Reaching AC7 therefore means editing real patient data, which this file will not do; AC6's required marking is asserted instead, and the manual step is one line for a PM. **THREE READINESS/LOCATOR TRAPS, each of which reads as the feature being absent:** the patient form's section header is **CSS-uppercased** — it paints `VERSICHERUNG` but its `textContent` stays `Versicherung`, and Playwright's text engine matches `textContent`, so `getByText('VERSICHERUNG', { exact: true })` never resolves and burns its whole timeout (the #3718/#3770 family; note `sa.patient-insurance-prefill.page.ts` still uses that locator); the patient route takes **no `?id=` query**; and on the VO form **"Speichern" paints EARLY** while the insurance section lands later, so a gate on it alone hands the caller a form whose fields are not there — this file's own AC1 test passed on one run and failed on the next with identical code until the gate waited for `Versicherungsart`. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.gkv-insurer-required.page.ts` |
| `sa_vo_delete_guardrails.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @VoDeletion @Security @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.14 (#3671, PR #3693) an Admin or Super Admin deletes a mistaken VO through a guarded confirmation step; the VO becomes recoverable for 30 days and appears on a new "Gelöscht" tab. **Deployed on both halves; 14 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **Deployment has to be decided twice** — `GET /status` reports `3.14.0` but that is the API only, so the first test reads the two preflight operations off the API AND the dialog's German out of the served entry bundle (#3705). **AC2's whole truth table is READ-ONLY**, which is what makes a destructive ticket testable: `GET /prescriptions/{id}/deletion-preflight` runs the SAME `PrescriptionDeletionService::resolveBlocker()` the confirm re-runs and writes nothing (the #3576 technique). All three blockers have live fixtures and each names its artifact — 5674-9 → `invoice_sent` "426-14", 4193-2 → `billing_batch` "E-2026-857-001", 3188-1 → `validated`. **The PRECEDENCE is measured and no AC states it:** 4207-2 is `validated` AND invoiced → reports `invoice_sent`; 4193-2 is `validated` AND batched → reports `billing_batch`, so the order is invoice → batch → validated and a QA reading the AC's table cannot predict which message such a VO shows. On screen: the action sits beside Speichern/Abbrechen; the summary's five rows; "Grund für das Löschen"; the 30-day sentence; the acknowledge checkbox with the confirm button `aria-disabled="true"` until it is ticked; the **blocked** dialog ("VO kann nicht gelöscht werden" + the batch message + "Schließen", no acknowledge behind it); AC5's warning interpolating the server-side count ("Diese VO hat 2 dokumentierte Behandlungen…"); AC6's parent warning naming 9634-10 with all three radios. AC15 is asserted by **x-order along the tab row** — the tabs are NOT `role="tab"` but leaf divs — giving `Folge-VO erhalten · Keine Folge-VO · Fertig behandelt · Zur Prüfung · Alle VOs · Alle inkl. Archivierte · Gelöscht`, with the badge equal to `dashboard-counts.deleted`. The one mutating test deletes and restores: status preserved (`Abgelaufen` → `Abgelaufen`), `restorableUntil` exactly `deletedAt + 30d`, one `prescription_deleted` Verlauf entry carrying who/when/why, the VO on the Gelöscht tab and searchable there, counts moving `allWithArchived −1 / deleted +1`, and a second delete answering 409. AC18 both ways: `treatmentStatus: "Gelöscht"` → **400 "Invalid treatment status"** while the control `Archiviert` → 200 (probed with an id matching nothing, #3561), and the frontend status enum's 10 cases carry no such label. AC9 server-side: no reason → 400, bogus reason → 400, `other` without a note → 422. AC1: a therapist gets 403 on the preflight and on `/delete`, and `exists[deletedAt]=true` **fails closed** for them (200 with `totalItems: 0`, not 403). **Traps:** `?patient=` is silently ignored on `/prescriptions` (returns the whole 34k book) — the registered filter is `patient.id=`; the VO form does **not remount** on a navigation between two edit URLs (#3535), so one form per test — `openVoForm()` waits on "Speichern" rather than the VO number, which renders as an exact leaf on some VOs and inside a larger string on others; and the Verlauf is permanent, so a fixture accumulates one delete/restore pair per run and a `find()` keeps returning the FIRST run's entry — assert the delta and the newest row. **FINDING** → reported: the **Gelöscht tab's empty state is in English** ("No prescriptions match these filters", "Clear filters", "Create VO") on an otherwise German board — shared with every other tab and pre-existing, but this is the tab that is empty by default, so it is where an admin reliably meets it (#3611's family). AC14 → `fixme`: `PrescriptionDeletionRecord` deliberately carries no `#[ApiResource]` (the DPO reads it from the database), so the permanent record has no client surface at all. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.vo-deletion.page.ts` |
| `sa_vo_deleted_exclusion.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @VoDeletedExclusion @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.14 (#3672, PR #3697) a deleted VO must disappear from every read surface in Flow, with ONE deliberate exception — the VO-number allocator, which must keep counting deleted VOs so a replacement never reuses a number. **Deployed; 3 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **The file is one round trip, and that shape is the point:** a surface that merely *does not contain* the VO proves nothing unless it contained it a moment earlier, so all twelve reads are taken three times — before → deleted → restored — on the same VO. Measured (VO 905301-1, patient 7826): patient VO list `3 → 2 → 3`, `/v2/prescriptions` the same, `dashboard-counts` `all 7017 → 7016 → 7017` / `allWithArchived 34266 → 34265 → 34266` / `deleted 0 → 1 → 0`, the Gelöscht tab `false → true → false`, both F.VO candidate lists `true → false → true`, `/activities?prescription[]=` `2 → 0 → 2`, the item read **200 for an admin throughout but `200 → 404 → 200` for the therapist** (which is the tablet arm from the other side), and the duplicate-check returning nothing. **AC4 in both directions: `next-id` answered `905301-4` in all three phases** — the number stayed reserved while the VO was deleted, which a central Doctrine SQL filter would have broken and is why the implementation used an API Platform read extension instead. **AC3 is a write that must FAIL, which is what makes it safe to run:** `POST /activities/bulk` against the deleted VO answers **422 `{"violations":[{"propertyPath":"prescription","message":"controls.activity.vo_deleted"}]}`** and writes nothing (re-checked). **Traps:** that endpoint takes a **bare JSON array** with a `therapist` IRI per row — wrapped in `{activities: […]}` it answers 400 "expected a JSON array" and a row without a therapist answers 400 too, either of which reads exactly like the constraint being absent, and `POST /activities` itself is **405**; `?patient=` is silently ignored (`patient.id=3` vs `patient=34266`, pinned by the first test); `/therapy_reports` registers **no per-VO filter at all** (only `prescription.patient` / `prescription.therapist`); and the i18n check must use the **leaf** key — `vo_deleted` occurs twice (de + en) while the full path `controls.activity.vo_deleted` occurs **zero** times, because the API returns it as the violation message and the frontend translates whatever it is handed, so a search for the dotted path reads as "never shipped". AC1's remaining four surfaces + AC2 → `fixme` with the reason per row: the tablet's own sync is device-side; CRM ordering cards and the KPI/Flow-Boards figures need a VO currently live in that surface's period (and KPI reads are cached ~5 min, #3401), i.e. real work somebody is doing; therapist notifications are generated by the nightly jobs. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.vo-deletion.page.ts` |
| `sa_vo_restore.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @VoRestore @Security @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.14 (#3674, PR #3702) restore a deleted VO within 30 days, with the follow-up re-linking step. **Deployed; 8 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **The round trip is genuinely lossless for a PARENT link, and the sharpest assertion is the DATE.** `PrescriptionDeletionService` snapshots `{parentId, parentFollowupStatus, parentReceivedDate, childId}` before it unwires anything; `relinkSnapshotParent()` puts the first three back and **suppresses `FollowUpStatusListener` for that one flush**, because the listener stamps the received date to NOW on any followupStatus change and would overwrite the very field "keep" exists to restore. Verified on 9634-11/9634-10: parent `received` + `2026-07-31T04:55:06+00:00` → after delete with `unchanged` **both null** (the #2539 reverse reset, correct) → after restore with `keep` **`received` and the ORIGINAL date to the millisecond**. Also verified: AC2 the exact pre-deletion status returns and no other field moves; AC4 a `prescription_restored` entry with who/when and the choice as a code + internal id; AC1 therapist 403 on both the preflight and `/restore`; the payload guards (unknown choice 422, `different` with no `parentId` 422, itself as parent 422, nonexistent parent 422) with the VO still deleted after every refusal; and the bundle carrying the dialog, all three choices and all three blocked messages — **plus `restore_pending_tooltip` at 0 occurrences, which is how you can tell #3674 landed on top of #3671 rather than the epic stopping at #3671's inert placeholder button.** **Traps:** `restore-preflight` answers **200 with `blocked: {type:"not_deleted"}`** on a live VO rather than 404, so a status check alone reads as "restore is available for every VO"; `restore()` resolves the BLOCKER before it validates the payload, so on a live VO every malformed body answers 409 `not_deleted` and a read-only payload-guard test measures the blocker and concludes the vocabulary is open — those guards are only reachable on a VO that really is deleted; and an omitted `parentChoice` defaults to `standalone`, silently dropping a parent link. **FINDING** → reported: **a restored VO never regains its follow-up CHILD link, although the deletion snapshot records `childId`.** Restore only ever re-links the parent, so deleting a VO that was itself a parent and restoring it leaves its follow-up permanently detached with nothing in either dialog saying so — #3671 AC7 announces the severing as informational, #3674 AC3 covers only the parent direction, and the epic's End Goal says the VO "returns exactly as it was". The field being written and never read is what makes it look unintended rather than decided. AC5 → `fixme`: `window_expired` needs a VO deleted more than 30 days ago and `deletedAt` cannot be back-dated from a client, so the earliest instance is 30 days after the first real deletion; the guard's other two codes, the window arithmetic and all three German messages are asserted instead. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.vo-deletion.page.ts` |
| `sa_insurance_pair_reprice.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @InsurancePairReprice @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3562) #3535 built confirm-and-reprice for PKV ↔ Privat Basis only; this widens the guard to EVERY insurance-type pair and adds a validation-status reset, superseding #3535's AC6. **Deployed (`d15d268e0` + `be7b69c53`); AC1 verified on real data, AC3 in the bundle, AC5 has never fired. 5 passed / 2 `fixme`.** **The headline is that #3535's repricing never actually ran, and this file proves it now does.** That guard compared the changeset value with `instanceof InsuranceTypeEnum`, but Doctrine hands back the **backing string**, so it was false on every correction and the pass was skipped with no error — exactly what this suite reported on #3535 ("the confirmation is live and gated exactly right; the repricing does not happen"). `be7b69c53`'s docblock now cites that diagnostic verbatim (`{"oldType":"string","newType":"string","oldIsEnum":false,...}`) on this very VO. **The evidence is a STRADDLING fixture, never a total.** VO **4355-1** has three PNF sessions either side of the PRIVAT_BASIS step on 2025-07-01, which separates the only three outcomes a reprice can have: priced at each session's own date → **47,69 / 45,92 / 45,92** (correct, AC1's "not today's date"); priced at today → 47,06 three times; never repriced → the old PKV 61,95 three times. It reads the first, each figure recomputed from the live price ladder rather than hardcoded, with a companion assertion that at least one session differs from today's price — otherwise "own date" and "today" would be indistinguishable on this data. **Reading the VO's total cannot tell these apart, and that is the #3535 false pass:** a VO's revenue is snapshotted treatment lines PLUS a live fee calculation, and only the fee half follows the current type, so `Gesamtumsatz` moves either way (4355-1 went 257,85 → 224,65 while still holding PKV session prices; fully repriced it is **178,33**, which is what it reads today). **Always read `ActivityTreatment.resolvedTariff`.** A third test ties the corrected prices to a CORRECTION rather than some other recompute: the VO's own log carries 11 insurance-type changes, **3 of them after the 2026-09-02 string fix**, the last leaving it on the type whose prices it now holds. AC3's dialog ships with the per-treatment-day wording ("Behandlungen neu bepreisen?", "der am jeweiligen Behandlungstag galt"). **FINDING — AC5's validation reset has NEVER fired on staging:** the reset stamps a `validation_status_change` log with reason "Validation reset after an insurance-type correction" and there are **0** of them; the PM records AC5 as PASS by code reading and their own session notes the dialog was "dismissed with Abbrechen to preserve test VO", so no correction was ever confirmed through the UI either. The reset also needs a non-null billing `validationStatus`, and the fixture's is null. **Also for the PM: their AC table is renumbered against the ticket** — their AC-3 describes the validation reset (the ticket's AC5), AC-6 the invoiced-VO guard (AC7), and AC-1/AC-2 the dialog (AC3) — so "7 of 7" does not map onto these seven ACs. AC2 → `fixme`: only ONE pair has been exercised on staging, and each other pair would need its own straddling fixture; the risk is concentrated in the guard (a single `$old !== $new`) rather than in the pairs, since `ActivityTreatmentSnapshotRecalculator` already maps all four tariff columns. AC5/AC6 → `fixme`: the reset is deliberately NOT self-restoring — changing the type back does not restore `validationStatus` — so a live run leaves a real VO unvalidated; AC6 follows from AC5 by construction. VO pool: public 24,142 / private 1,299 / accident 31 / privat_basis 10. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.insurance-pair-reprice.page.ts` |
| `sa_reactivation_notice.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ReactivationNotice @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3561, PR #3585) reactivating an expired VO (Abgelaufen → Aktiv) whose Folge-VO Status is "Bestellen" already clears that status and the Bestelldatum (#3197) — nothing told the admin. This ticket adds the notice: per VO on a single reactivation, one summary with a count on a bulk one. The clearing itself is unchanged. **Deployed; every client-inspectable half verified. 5 passed / 2 `fixme`.** **Both deployed surfaces can be inspected WITHOUT reactivating anything, which matters because a reactivation is not reversible in the way that counts** — setting the VO back to Abgelaufen does not restore the `followupStatus`/`orderDate` it destroyed, so every live run would permanently consume a fixture. (1) `POST /prescriptions/status/bulk` now answers **`{count, clearedFollowupCount}`**, the number the summary renders; **a payload naming an id that matches nothing probes it with zero side effects** — `findBy(['id' => …])` returns an empty set and the loop body never runs, giving a live `{"count":0,"clearedFollowupCount":0}`. **That distinction is load-bearing:** the loop writes a `treatment_status_change` log for EVERY prescription it matches, even when the status is unchanged, so the tempting "post Aktiv onto an already-Aktiv VO" probe is NOT side-effect free and is deliberately avoided. (2) The single-VO notice is decided client-side by `reactivationClearedFollowup()` reading the **PATCH response** rather than recomputing — so it cannot report a clearing that did not happen — and its contract is that a cleared `followupStatus` is **omitted from the payload entirely** rather than serialized null (#3302); asserted here on both states (three Abgelaufen+`order` VOs serialize `"order"`; a VO without it omits the key). **One i18n pair serves both paths** — `reactivation_cleared_followup_count_one`/`_other` interpolating `{{count}}`, so a single reactivation renders the SINGULAR of the string the bulk summary uses ("bei 1 reaktivierten VO"); both keys ship ×2 (de + en) and the German values are present in escaped form (#3611), with the frontend confirmed to reference `clearedFollowupCount`. AC5 holds by construction: the count arrives in the response body, i.e. after the write is committed, so nothing can gate the reactivation. **Fixture pool measured live: 2,107 Abgelaufen VOs, 983 with Folge-VO Status "Bestellen", 166 with BOTH** — and the ticket's own named example, **VO 5708 (1284-17)**, is back in that state, having been reactivated on 31 Aug, cleared, expired again and re-acquired "Bestellen". **Undocumented surface worth QA time:** the developer flagged on the PR that the notice also covers the **dashboards' inline VO-status pills**, not just the edit form — that is in neither the ticket body nor the ACs. AC1/AC2 and AC3 → `fixme`: they need real reactivations, each consuming a fixture irreversibly. Manual steps, including the "clear the dropdown yourself in the same save" case the helper deliberately keeps quiet, are in `docs/manual-test-3561-reactivation-notice.md`. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.reactivation-notice.page.ts` |
| `sa_price_change_invoice_impact.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PriceChangeImpact @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3650) a back-dated Heilmittel price must warn, before saving, which ALREADY-ISSUED invoices would change amount — a back-dated entry earlier in 2026 silently moved **65 issued copayment invoices** (122,96 EUR, written off) — and must refresh Not Sent drafts after the save. **Deployed; AC1, AC3 and AC4 verified live. 5 passed / 2 `fixme`.** **The preflight is an unusually good surface: EXACT and non-writing.** `POST /treatments/price-change-invoice-impact` (a plain Symfony route with `#[IsGranted('ROLE_ADMIN')]`, because `use_symfony_listeners: false` means a custom controller never reaches an API Platform `security:` expression — #3529) INSERTS the candidate `treatment_price_history` rows, runs the **real** `ActivityTreatmentSnapshotRecalculator`, reads the new amounts and rolls the transaction back — so the warning's figures are the figures a save would produce, and a test may fire it freely. A test re-reads the price history afterwards to prove the rollback (20 rows before, 20 after, candidate gone). Payload `{entries:[{treatmentId, tariffType, effectiveDate, price}]}`, max **200**. Verified live with KG (id 71) PRIVAT 99,00 from 2026-01-01: **24 issued invoices**, each carrying AC1's four columns, every one actually moving amount, and **no patient field anywhere** in the response (the #3325 GDPR shape it mirrors). AC4 verified on all three no-warning paths — effective today, effective in the future, and back-dated at the price already in force — all `count: 0`. **FINDING — AC3's table is not the implemented rule.** The AC names six included statuses; the code uses `InvoiceStatusEnum::issued()` = *everything except NOT_SENT and CANCELLED*, so it also covers statuses the table never mentions: the live warning returns **R426-64, status `on_hold` (Pausiert)**. Defensible (an on-hold invoice has been issued) but unsanctioned by the AC, and a QA reading the table literally would file it. **A second divergence, deliberate and worth keeping:** a **NOT_SENT invoice that HAS been DATEV-synced** is listed although AC3 says Not Sent → No — because the after-save draft refresh is draft-tier only (NOT_SENT **and** never synced), so a synced draft would otherwise fall between the two halves of the ticket and be repriced with no warning. Staging has exactly one, **R126-86**, and the warning lists it. Stornos are excluded by `originalInvoice IS NULL`. **Trap:** `tariffType` is the `TreatmentTariffType` enum (`GKV`/`PRIVAT`/`PRIVAT_BASIS`/`BEIHILFE`/`BG`), **not** an insurance type — `PKV` is rejected **400**, a welcome change from the silently-ignored filters elsewhere in this API. AC5 → `fixme`: it needs the real save, and although `POST`+`DELETE` on `/treatment_price_histories` is exactly self-restoring for #3378's `resolvedTariff`, this ticket adds a SECOND effect to that round trip (the draft re-snapshot) and nothing establishes that the DELETE reverts a draft amount too — a repriced draft would be left on real billing data. The arithmetic is nevertheless verified, since `recomputedAmount` comes from the same recalculator the save runs. AC2/AC6 → `fixme` (dialog-level, needs a real write; AC6 holds by construction because the preflight writes nothing, and the frozen-amount guarantee itself is #3093's issue-time snapshot, asserted in `sa_legacy_invoice_amount_lock.spec.ts`). **Manual QA steps** — including the fixture that exercises AC5 (KG on the **GKV** tariff, prescribed on **55 VOs carrying a Not Sent copayment draft**) — are in `docs/manual-test-3650-backdated-price-warning.md`. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.price-change-impact.page.ts` |
| `sa_bulk_mark_sent_scope.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @BulkMarkSent @Security @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.13 (#3549, PR #3688) `POST /prescriptions/report-send/bulk` marked therapy reports "sent" for **arbitrary VO ids** — a bare `findBy` on the posted ids with no therapist predicate, while every sibling bulk endpoint (organizer, transfer, ordering-status) already narrowed non-admins to their own caseload. A **P1 write-integrity** finding from the SEC-05.4 audit (#3469), out of that task's scope as a non-read gap. **Deployed as `13f109033` (2026-09-11); both ACs verified live. 6 passed.** **The write side was the worse half and the tests assert it:** every marked report also persisted a `PrescriptionLog` attributed to the CALLER against a VO they do not own — a forged audit trail, not merely an unauthorised state change — so the foreign-VO assertions check the log count, not just the report's `sent` flag. Verified live: therapist 198 posting `{id:[34085 own, 29949 foreign]}` → **`{count: 1}`**, own report marked with exactly one entry attributed to Jhenqa Test, **foreign report still unsent with 0 entries**. **THE TEST-DESIGN TRAP, which makes a broken build look fixed:** the query also filters `tr.sentAt IS NULL AND tr.archivedDate IS NULL`, so a foreign report that is ALREADY SENT is excluded for a reason unrelated to authorization and a count of 0 proves nothing — every fixture is verified unsent+unarchived first, and **AC2's control re-posts the very same id as an admin** (→ `{count: 1}`, marked by SA Jhen), which is what rules out "the report was ineligible anyway" and turns the therapist's zero into evidence of scoping. **Silent exclusion is correct, not a weak refusal** — it matches `BulkPostOrganizerController` deliberately, because a 403 would break the partial-batch contract AND confirm that a VO id exists; a test pins that a **nonexistent** id and a **foreign** id are byte-identical in the response (both `200 count=0`), so no id is ever confirmed to exist. **Run shape:** `--grep "@ReadOnly"` runs the safe four, *including the central security test* — posting a foreign id alone writes NOTHING while the fix holds, and if it ever writes, that IS the vulnerability; `@Mutating` adds the two that consume fixtures, because marking a report sent is forward-only with no client-reachable undo (pools measured at run time: caller 11, foreign therapist 7 has 15, and both tests skip rather than fail when exhausted). Also asserted: the payload guards reject before any write (`400 Invalid payload: "id" (array) is required.`, and `Batch size cannot exceed 500 items.` at `MAX_BULK_IDS + 1`). A read-only audit sweep checks the invariant the bug would break — every `therapyReportStatus` entry was written by the VO's own therapist or an admin (5 of 5 in the newest 3,000 `field_change` logs: 2 by the owner, 3 by admins) — and it is deliberately **bounded, not a census**: the collection holds **463,418** `field_change` rows and the `therapyReportStatus` marker lives only in the meta, which no filter reaches. **Traps:** `/therapy_reports` registers only `prescription.therapist` and `prescription.patient` — there is no `sent`/`archived` filter, so the pool is narrowed client-side; and an embedded `therapist` carries `@id` but **no bare `id`** (owner comparison goes through `fullName`, which is why a first pass printed `owner None` for every row). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.bulk-mark-sent-scope.page.ts` |
| `sa_reminder_letter.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ReminderLetter @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3559) an Overdue PKV or Privat Basis invoice gains a ready-to-send Zahlungserinnerung (singly and in bulk) and the automatic Reminded → To Debt Collector window moves from 7 days to **14**. **Deployed; AC1, AC3, AC4, AC6, AC7, AC8, AC9 and AC10 verified live. 8 passed / 2 `fixme`.** **Downloading a letter writes NOTHING, and that is asserted rather than assumed** — the ticket's PM notes say "remindedDate field stamped once on download", which would be a defect (it would mark the invoice Reminded before the admin sent anything, starting the 14-day debt-collection clock early, and would contradict the ticket's own "does NOT change the manual action admins already use to mark an invoice Reminded"). `ReminderLetterDownloadController` checks the audit voter, calls `isReminderLetterAvailable()`, renders and returns — no `persist`, no `flush`, same for the bulk controller and the renderer — and a test re-reads the fixture after BOTH downloads to prove `status`/`remindedDate` are untouched. The guard works off the EXISTING manual stamp, exactly as specified. **`Invoice::isReminderLetterAvailable()` is three ACs in one line** — `status === OVERDUE` (AC1/AC9) `&& remindedDate === null` (AC5) `&& insuranceType.usesPkvBilling()` (AC2; copayment/GKV never — AC10) — and both endpoints apply it, so the bulk path cannot diverge; a test ports it and compares against the endpoint over a sample spanning both outcomes (8/8 agree). **AC6/AC7/AC8 are proven by a natural experiment in the log, which is strictly stronger than a status check** (a status check cannot tell "moved at 14" from "moved at 7 and nobody looked"): three seeded invoices sat at Reminded with different ages when the rule shipped — **651** (reminded 2026-08-11, **30 days**) and **654** (2026-08-27, **exactly 14 days**) both moved automatically in the SAME nightly run at 2026-09-10T00:56:34 (AC8 + AC6), while **655** (2026-08-28, **13 days** that night) did **not** move and went on the NEXT run, 2026-09-11, at exactly 14 days (AC7). Two invoices one day apart, two consecutive runs. AC4 is read out of the rendered PDF: invoice number, date, `Ursprüngliches Zahlungsziel`, open amount, patient billing address, the Gesellschaft's frozen name/IBAN/BIC, the full Inkasso warning, and a new deadline that is **generation date + 14** — checked against the letter's own printed dates rather than a clock the test reads. AC3: 3 eligible invoices → 3 PDFs in one zip, and an ineligible copayment id is **reported in `Fehlerbericht.txt`** ("Für diese Rechnungen ist keine Zahlungserinnerung verfügbar…") rather than silently dropped (#3495's convention). **Traps:** the bulk payload key is **`id`**, not `invoiceIds` (#3333's shape) — `invoiceIds` answers `400 Invalid payload: "id" (array) is required`; **`/invoices?id[]=` is SILENTLY IGNORED** and returns the whole 587-row book, so a batched "fetch these invoices" helper reports whatever the first page holds (`/prescriptions?id[]=` and `/patients?id[]=` DO work — the convention does not carry across collections, and a first pass here produced a bogus treatment-status distribution because of it); `remindedDate`/`totalOnHoldDays` are DEFAULT-group only while `invoiceType` is `invoice-list:read` only (#3604); and the transition is `>= 14` **minus `totalOnHoldDays`**, so "exactly 14" only reads cleanly at zero on-hold days. **`Pages/util/pdf-text.ts` drops a line of this letter** — `pdfText()` renders the Inkasso sentence as "…die Forderung an ein gestellt.", losing "Inkassounternehmen zu übergeben. Die dadurch entstehenden Kosten werden Ihnen zusätzlich in Rechnung"; `pdfDocText()` from `pdf-layout.ts` recovers it and the Twig template has it, so **the letter is complete and the extractor is at fault** — use `pdfDocText` for this document, and treat a missing line from `pdfText()` as suspect before reporting it as a rendering defect. **FINDING** → the PM's own open question ("PKV tab Überfällig filter shows (0) despite 118 overdue invoices … needs separate investigation") is answered and is NOT a filter bug: `pkvBilling[invoiceStatus]=overdue` returns **0** and with `treatmentStatus=Archiviert` returns **114** — nearly every overdue invoice sits on an archived VO, which the billing list hides by default (#3277). Same seam that empties #3648's Fehler tab. **Also for the PM:** their AC table is misaligned with the ticket — the AC-2 row describes the letter's Gesellschaft header and AC-3 its content (both AC4's subject), AC-9 describes the PKV-only guard (AC10) and AC-10 a "Reminded Date" column no AC mentions — so "10 of 10" covers fewer than ten distinct ACs, and the two genuinely uncovered ones are not among the rows marked PASS. AC2 → `fixme`: of 118 overdue invoices **114 are `private`, 4 `public`, ZERO `privat_basis`**, so that branch is unexercised on staging (a data gap, not a suspected defect — the rule reads the enum, and #3534 established Privat Basis follows the PKV rules). AC5 → `fixme`: it needs `overdue` AND `remindedDate != null` (the manual-correction flip-back the docblock calls out); staging's ONLY overdue invoice with a remindedDate is R426-68, which is copayment, so its 409 is already explained by AC10 and cannot attribute the remindedDate branch — and producing one means marking a real PKV invoice Reminded, which starts its 14-day clock. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.reminder-letter.page.ts` |
| `sa_activate_owner_parity.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ActivateOwnerParity @Security @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3550, PR #3689) `PATCH /prescriptions/{id}/activate` must enforce owner-or-admin like `/terminate`. A P2 follow-up from the SEC-05.4 audit (#3469): that task gave activate a care-relationship gate (unrelated caller → **404**, existence non-disclosure) but left a residual — an **in-scope non-owner**, a therapist covering the patient via a `SharedPatient` share, could still activate someone else's follow-up VO. **Deployed (`e6d49b03c`); all three AC clauses verified live, 5 passed.** **The ticket is a four-leg matrix and this file drives it on BOTH endpoints**, which the title ("owner-parity with terminate") asks for and the PM's notes do not cover: unrelated **404 / 403**, in-scope non-owner **403 / 403**, owner **400 / 400**, admin **400 / 400** (activate / terminate). **Every leg sends an EMPTY body, which is what makes a security test on two write endpoints safe in CI** — both controllers check authorization *before* parsing the payload and both reject `{}` (activate wants `activate`, terminate wants `immediate` + `reasons`), so "reached the body" registers as 400 and no VO is ever activated or terminated; a final test re-reads all three fixture VOs and proves `treatmentStatus`/`followupStatus` did not move. **The insight the file re-proves rather than trusting:** the dev's PR note says a test built like terminate's would have proven nothing here, because terminate's test uses an *unrelated* colleague and on activate that caller is stopped by the 404 gate before reaching the new rule — leg 1 demonstrates exactly that (unrelated → 404, never 403), so the in-scope fixture is the only way to reach the code under test. **Fixtures are discovered, not hardcoded, and use only the suite's own therapist login:** the PM verified with four accounts whose passwords they had reset for that session (users 7/8/15 on VO 3188-1), which is neither reproducible in CI nor ours to depend on; `/shared_patients` rows read `{ownerTherapist: <sharer>, therapist: <shared-with>, patient}`, so a row whose `therapist` is the caller puts them in scope for a patient whose VOs belong to someone else. Both routes were run and agree leg for leg. **Two silent-filter traps on `/prescriptions`, pointing OPPOSITE ways** — an unregistered filter returns the unfiltered 34,265 rows, which reads exactly like "this patient has every VO": by patient the registered name is **`patient.id=`** (`patient=`/`patientId=` ignored), by therapist it is **`therapist=`** (`therapist.id=`/`therapists.id=` ignored) — and that is the mirror image of `/activities`, where `prescription=` works and `prescription.id=` is ignored (#3533); neither collection's convention predicts the other's, so `assertFiltersPartition()` proves both before any count is believed. Also asserted: the refusal text is generic and contains no patient id, owner id or VO number. **FINDING** → reported, not failed: **the parity is one-directional.** An unrelated caller gets **404 on activate but 403 on terminate**, because `TerminatePrescriptionController` has no care gate at all (no `CareRelationshipChecker`, no 404 path) — so terminate confirms the VO exists to a therapist with no relationship to the patient. `authorization-matrix.md` marks that row **OK-justified** because owner-or-admin is "stricter than care relation", which is true of who may ACT but does not address who may learn the VO exists — the property #3469 established for the sibling endpoint. A decision for the audit owner. **Doc nit:** the matrix cites the owner-parity commit as `0f0eb3a43`, which **diverged** from `release/3.13.0` (pre-rebase of the stacked PR); the commit that shipped is `e6d49b03c`. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.activate-owner-parity.page.ts` |
| `sa_fee_agreement_upload.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @FeeAgreementUpload @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.14 (#3790, commit `0d5bc0d27`, on `release/3.14.0` AND `release/3.15.0`, no PR) an uploaded signed Honorarvereinbarung must count as the patient's signed fee agreement everywhere. **Deployed and verified end to end; 7 passed, 0 `fixme`.** Uploading a signed fee agreement created only a `PatientDocument` and never touched the patient's `Hono`, so the Document Center, the waiting PKV VOs and the ETI contract date all still saw an unsigned agreement. `HonoUploadSigner` now moves the patient's open Hono to SIGNED — **as a status UPDATE on purpose**, because `HonoStatusListener` reacts to updates only and is the one place that stamps `signedDate`, writes the Hono log and activates the waiting PKV VOs; a Hono persisted as SIGNED would skip all three. **THE TRAP, and it silently tests the wrong person:** the ticket says "patient 8789 / 8786 / 9498", and those are the **UI patient NUMBER**, not the API id — they resolve to **7517 / 7522 / 8286**, and `GET /patients/8789` answers **200 for an unrelated patient**, so a spec written on the ticket's numbers finds none of the described state and reads as "not reproducible" (#3577's shape; the mapping is pinned in its own test, resolved through a Document Center row's `patientNumber`). **Everything the ACs name is readable and only the trigger is a write:** the fee-agreements card is `GET /document_center/documents?type=hv&status=…` (**`type=hv`**, not `honorarvereinbarung`, which answers 0), its open count is `hvActionable` on `/document_center/counts`, the Hono carries `status`/`signedDate`/`logs`/`honoDocumentId`/`pkvPrescriptionCount`, and AC3's tile is the `honoFehlt` rows of `/kpis/admin-performance/risks`. Verified read-only at full scale: **AC1's invariant over every signed fee-agreement document on staging** — a patient holding one must have a signed live Hono — partitioned on the fix's ship time, because the ticket does NOT backfill (**1 historical divergence from 2026-05-12, patient 7081, both Honos archived; 0 after the cutover**, the #3651/#3709 rule); and **AC3 over the whole tile**, 0 VOs of a signed-document patient on `honoFehlt`, which is the "already worked" half since `HonoStatusChecker` and `findHonoFehlt()` read the DOCUMENT. **The end-to-end test is env-gated because the upload cannot be undone** — `DELETE /patient_documents/{id}` is **405**, no delete route exists, and the request also archives the patient's unsigned Honos, signs one and moves PKV VOs to Aktiv. Run once on **patient 8470 `Sanji VinsmokeTest`** (deliberately a QA patient, not the ticket's real case): Hono 622 `not_sent` → **`signed` + signedDate + log**, VOs **965403-2 and 965403-3 both For Review → Aktiv** each with the `Honorarvereinbarung unterschrieben` entry, the Document Center row `hv-622` → `signed` with its `signedAt` and file, and both VOs off the tile — signed HV documents went **91 → 92** and honoFehlt rows **105 → 104** on the very next read. **That fixture also gives AC2's *Not sent* row and a free scope control**: its GKV VO 965403-1 is asserted UNTOUCHED, since the ticket's Out of Scope says only PKV VOs wait. **More traps:** the multipart create is **`POST /patient_documents/upload`**, its own `uriTemplate` — posting to the collection answers **405**, which reads exactly like the route being gone; **`?patient=` works on `/honos` and is SILENTLY IGNORED on `/patient_documents`** (it returns all 965, and the registered filter there is `documentType`, with `type` ignored), so neither collection's convention predicts the other's (#3550) and `assertFiltersPartition()` proves each before a count is believed; and **`signedDate` is OMITTED when unset**, so it reads `undefined` and a `toBeNull()` fails on exactly the state being pinned (#3302/#3709). **Deployment needed a bundle probe:** the API half adds no route and no serialized field, so it has no read-only probe and `/status` answers for neither the commit (#3704) nor the frontend (#3705) — but the app half ships in the same commit and its invalidation block survives minification verbatim (`'honorarvereinbarung'===O&&(k.invalidateQueries({queryKey:['honos']}),…)`), and the served entry bundle changed the same morning. **Patient 8286 (the ticket's "9498") is pinned in its reported state and deliberately never written to** — it is the billing team's real case and the last untouched instance, and the ticket itself records that its other repro patient was consumed by a workaround run. AC2's *Sent* and *no/archived-only* rows, AC3's new-VO clause and the ETI contract date → `docs/manual-test-3790-fee-agreement-upload.md` (the first two need another irreversible upload, the ETI one would file a real claim — this suite never submits, per #3473). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.fee-agreement-upload.page.ts` |
| `sa_working_hours_abrechnung.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @WorkingHoursAbrechnung @ReadOnly` | RC 3.15 (#3725, PR #3780 merged into `release/3.15.0` 2026-09-22) the Therapeuten-Orga **Arbeitszeiten** table gains a third column mode, **Abrechnung**, with five per-therapist VO counts and an expandable per-facility breakdown. **Deployed; all six ACs verified, 8 passed, 0 `fixme`. Read-only** — every request a GET; the board is navigated, switched and expanded. **AC5 is the whole ticket** (“the facility sub-rows sum to the therapist row”) and it is exactly checkable, because the board's own `GET /kpis/management/working-hours` carries BOTH sides — so the payload is captured as the table loads (#3471) rather than re-issued, and the painted numbers are compared against the payload they were drawn from. **Measured over the whole population: 100 therapists with a breakdown, 0 mismatches on all five columns**, plus the painted sub-rows summing to the painted row on screen, iterated column by column so a sixth count added later cannot be left out of the invariant. **THE HEADLINE TRAP, and it is a cross-ticket one: the payload carries THREE completed-ish fields and only one of them satisfies AC5.** `completedUnbilledCount` — which PR #3780's own body says it “reused” — is now the **UNION of all three end statuses**, because **#3775 widened it the very next day** (`0f909db2a` changed that field from 3 to 5 in #3725's own integration test); the identity `completedUnbilledCount == completedOnly + cancelled + expired` holds on **100/100** rows. Binding the column to it would **break AC5 on 77 of 100 therapists** and triple-count against the Abgebrochen/Abgelaufen columns beside it — measured, so the test can fail. The frontend correctly reads **`completedOnlyUnbilledCount`** with a comment naming #3775, and the discriminating fixture is chosen live (a therapist where the two fields differ — **Andreas Rosky, 28 against 36**), because a therapist where they agree proves nothing. **FINDING** → reported as a green test: **the two “Fertig” columns disagree, and the one that is supposed to be a SUBSET reads LARGER on 77 of 105 therapists** — including **5 where Abrechnung reads 0 and Operativ does not** (Karsten Jahns 0 against 8). AC3 calls the new column “a different, therapist-level breakdown” and the PR records the only difference as the >30-day staleness filter; they now differ on **two** axes, since Operativ's `completedUnbilledOver30Count` is the stale subset of the **union** while Abrechnung's is the whole unbilled population of **Fertig Behandelt alone**. Each is correct under its own definition, so it is a PM decision — but the labels differ by one word (`Fertig n. abger.` vs `Fertig (n. abger.)`) and a TO user switching modes sees the subset exceed the total. **That one-word difference is also AC3's own trap:** the two headers differ ONLY by their parentheses, so an `includes` matches both and AC3 passes on a build still rendering the old column — the check is exact-membership, **in both directions** (Abrechnung has the new and not the old; Operativ the old and not the new), which also stops the assertion being satisfied by an empty header row. **TWO MORE READING TRAPS, each of which reads as the feature being absent.** (a) **The board opens in GRUPPEN view, whose rows are `team-toggle-g<teamId>`, not `working-hours-name-t<id>`** (#3718) — polling for the therapist id never resolves until the view is switched, and the 240 s timeout then looks exactly like the whole mode missing (that is how this file first failed); readiness accepts either, with a separate therapist-row gate after the switch. (b) **The headers are CSS-uppercased**, so the ticket and the PR quote `AKTIVE VOS` (which is `innerText`) while a DOM read returns `Aktive VOs` — a constant written in the painted form fails on a correct build; both readings are asserted, and `Maßnahme` survives the source reading where `innerText` gives `MASSNAHME` (#3718/#3770's ß→SS rule). Also verified: **AC1** the toggle offers **Details · Operativ · Abrechnung** — note #3770 renamed Standard to **Details**, so the PR's own evidence line (“Standard · Operativ · Abrechnung”) no longer matches and `Standard` is asserted GONE; **AC2** 12 painted rows agreeing with the payload field for field; **AC4** one sub-row per facility under `working-hours-facility-t<id>-fac-<facilityId>` with `├─`/`└─` connectors; **AC6** collapse removing both the sub-rows and their connectors; the **facility-less bucket** rendering as **`Ohne Einrichtung`** (`facilityId: null`, 5 of 105 therapists) — not an AC, but load-bearing for AC5 since `elderly_care_home_id` is nullable and dropping those rows would break the sum for anyone treating outside a facility; and **the PR's own browser-only defect**, that the sub-row numbers must share the therapist row's right edge — it records fixing a left-aligned render that made a correct breakdown read as broken, and that its unit tests asserted values rather than geometry and passed throughout, so the geometry is re-measured here (all three sub-rows and the therapist row at `[742, 878, 1014, 1150, 1286]`). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.working-hours-abrechnung.page.ts` |
| `sa_search_vo_number_sort.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @SearchVoSort @ReadOnly` | RC 3.15 (#3872, PR #3883 merged into `release/3.15.0` 2026-09-30) while a search is active the Admin Board sorts results by **VO number, highest first** instead of by issue date — patient number then sequence, both as integers, so **9018-10 ranks above 9018-9**. **Deployed on both halves; all seven ACs verified, 11 passed, 0 `fixme`. Read-only** — every request a GET; the board is navigated, searched, sorted and read. On a copy of production the old order disagreed with VO order for **461 of 5,602 patients**. **The ticket is a sort order, so everything turns on telling the shipped order from the one it replaces, and TWO oracles do that.** (a) On the API, `order[voNumber]` against a **lexicographic** sort of the same rows — the orders are DISJOINT wherever a patient has a two-digit sequence, since `-10` ranks between `-1` and `-2` lexicographically and above `-9` numerically. Each fixture carries an **anti-vacuity guard requiring the two rules to disagree**, because on a patient with no two-digit sequence they agree and the comparison proves nothing. (b) On the board, the `order[…]` key in its **OWN request**, which IS `resolveSort()`'s output — `order[voNumber]=desc` while searching, `order[date]=desc` otherwise. **THE FIXTURES ARE NOT THE OBVIOUS ONES, because the board hides archived VOs** (`exclude[treatmentStatus][]=Archiviert`): the ticket's own screenshot patient **9018 has just 2 VOs on staging**, and **2960 — which holds -19 down to -3 — is archived in its entirety, so the board shows 0 rows for it**. Scanning the 7,414 board-visible rows gives **43** patients carrying both a ≥10 and a ≤9 sequence; the file uses **7354** (`-14 … -9`, six rows on one page with `-10` directly above `-9`) and **9634** (24 rows, `9634-99/17/16/15/13/11/10/5…1` plus four sibling patients — maximally discriminating), with **901** (29 VOs across **15 patients**, 3 pages) for AC2 row 3 and AC1's cross-page clause. Verified: AC1/AC2 all three rows; AC1's “across all pages” as monotonicity over three pages with no row repeated; AC3 clearing restores issue date; AC4 a header wins and its **third click returns to VO order**; AC5 a chosen column survives typing AND clearing a search; AC6 on **all seven tabs including Gelöscht** and at phone width; AC7 the arrow moving onto `VO #` and off `Ausst. Datum`. **FOUR TRAPS, and three of them fail a CORRECT build.** (1) **A repeat of a query the client already holds fires NO request at all** — React Query serves it from cache, and that is exactly what AC4's third click, a click on the already-active tab and AC3's clear all do; asserting a request there fails on a working board (it did here twice). The **arrow and the painted rows** are the signals that survive a cache hit, so AC3 additionally compares the painted rows against what the API serves for `order[date]=desc` — without that it would pass on a board that kept VO order and merely repainted the arrow. (2) **A search that matches nothing paints no table and therefore no header row**, so the arrow reads `null` — `Zur Prüfung` does this for the `901` search, which is not the feature failing; the arrow is asserted only where a table painted, with a count requiring several to have done so. (3) **The glyph follows the DIRECTION** — `↑` for asc, `↓` for desc, `↕` for “sortable, not active” — so asserting `↓` on an ascending column fails on a correct build. (4) An unknown `order` key is **silently ignored** (#3449), so “the filter exists” has to be *asc differs from desc* against that control, never a 200. Also: a **`socket hang up`** throws before any status exists, so the API helper retries on a thrown transport error and not only on a 5xx. **Two things the ACs do not cover and this file pins.** The PR's **malformed-VO-number decision**: the `uniqid('VO-')` fallback, unmatched imports and #3675's nulled numbers sort **last in BOTH directions** — verified on the real head and tail of all 35,067 rows (`ZZP…`/`ETITEST…` fill both tails, never a head). And a **collateral fix**: PR #3883 also repaired a pre-existing **500** on `/v2/prescriptions?order[orderingStatus]=…` whenever the request also carried a join — which **every real Admin Board search does**, since `SearchOrFilter` left-joins `patient` — because API Platform's `QueryChecker` misparsed the inlined `CASE WHEN o.orderingStatus …` as the alias `"CASE WHEN o"`; the joined combination now answers 200 and #3749's ranked order still works without a search. **The Out of Scope is checked mechanically rather than argued:** the filter exists on `/v2/prescriptions` and nowhere else, so the v1 `/prescriptions` path — which the Billing Validation Queue and every other searchable list read — **ignores `order[voNumber]` exactly like an unknown key** and cannot inherit VO order. **Also corrected here:** `AdminDashboardPage.search()`'s docblock claimed the search box goes `readonly` after submitting and offers no clear control; re-measured, it stays editable and grows a **“✕”**, which is AC3's own first route out. Uses `Pages/admin/admin.search-vo-sort.page.ts` |
| `sa_blanko_billing_validation.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @BlankoBillingValidation @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.15 (**#3841** the units check must stop failing Blanko VOs + **#3842** the mapping check must accept any Blanko position of the VO's own therapy area), both shipped in commit `646440999` (`Ref #3841 #3842`, **no PR**) on `release/3.15.0`. **Both deployed and working; 10 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **The fix is in the EVALUATOR, not the config, and the registry proves it:** checks **17 `billed_units_not_exceed_prescribed`** and **30 `service_maps_to_remedy`** still list `blanko` in `applicableVoKinds`, so a probe that reads the validation registry — the authoritative surface for #3708 and #3576 — concludes nothing shipped. The first test pins that deliberate non-change so nobody re-derives it as a missing deploy. **THE BEFORE/AFTER IS ON RECORD AND NEEDS NO WRITE,** which is the whole method here: a stored `PrescriptionValidation` row is the judgement made when the VO was last validated, so on a VO nobody has re-opened since the deploy it is literally the **pre-fix answer**. **13 Blanko VOs carried a stored `passed: false` for check 30 when this was first measured (2026-10-02) and the deployed rule disagrees with every one of them** — and every one fails on an **archived** position (`PFB-H-BV`, `KG-H-BV`, `MFB-H-BV`, `NOB-H-BV`), which is exactly the case #3842 AC1's second row calls out ("including Blanko positions no longer in the current catalog"). The read-only test asserts the durable invariant (*no stored check-30 failure on a Blanko VO survives today's rule*) rather than a count, so it stays green as the pool drains. **AC2 is then shown end to end on one of them:** the re-evaluation **updates the verdict row in place** — same id, `false → true` — with `validationStatus`/`creationValidationStatus`/`treatmentStatus` untouched, **nothing removed**, and a second run adding nothing. The row count does grow (29 → 33 on VO 6742-1) and that is understood rather than tolerated: the added rows are **checks 51/52/53/55**, registered after that VO was last validated, so a re-evaluation writes a verdict for every *currently* applicable check — a one-time catch-up, asserted as "every added row is for a check that had no verdict before". **#3841 AC3 reproduces the ticket's own table literally:** `8978-1` (created in Flow, KG-BV **11 delivered / 0 prescribed**) and `8603-2` (TheOrg import, **MT-BV 6/1 and KG-BV 6/1**) would both fail under the ported pre-fix rule and both pass now. **THE TRAP THAT INVENTS PRE-FIX FAILURES THAT NEVER HAPPENED, and it cost a pass here:** the units check counts **delivered** sessions, not `ActivityTreatment` rows. A planned session and a rejected-without-signature one both carry attachments, so a raw count runs ahead of the real one — VO **6381-4** reads **11 attachments against 10 prescribed KG-H** and the check correctly passes it, because only 10 were delivered. An oracle on `billedCounts()` therefore reports a working check as broken *and* fabricates pre-fix failures; `deliveredCounts()` applies #3649's `isDeliveredSession()`, and the fields are **`rejectedTreatment` / `rejectedTreatmentWithSignature`** — the shorter `rejected` / `rejectedWithSignature` do not exist on an Activity, so a predicate on them excludes nothing and silently degrades back to the raw count. **What the corrected oracle then shows is the actual shape of the bug:** a Blanko VO's budget is not a session count. Most Blanko VOs prescribe **no treatment-kind position at all** (26 of 30 sampled — their list is fees: `VBP-BV` is a one-time fee per #3712, `AEB-BV` is passiv), the rest carry either **0** units (built in Flow) or a number TheOrg brought with it — so the pre-fix rule was never off by one, it compared against a number the VO does not answer. **AC4's failing row has NO staging fixture and the reason is measured, not assumed:** a walk of **460 standard Fertig Behandelt VOs found 0 delivering past their budget** (the Doku flow will not document beyond it) and **0 prescribing zero units**, so every over-budget VO on staging is a Blanko one — which is the scope statement AC4 is really making. Its second line *is* exercised: `6381-4` sits at **10/10** and passes. **#3842 AC3 carries the sharpest control in the file — `6975-5` prescribes KG and bills `KG-BV`, an own-area BV position, EXACTLY the shape #3842 now accepts on a Blanko VO, and it must still fail.** It does, with the shipped note *"Abgerechnete Leistung KG-BV ist nicht auf der VO verordnet."* — so the new branch is demonstrably gated on `blankoVO` and not merely on the position being BV; a standard VO billing a plain unlisted `KG` (`1280-28`) sits beside it. **More traps:** `numberOfTreatments` is serialized as a **STRING**, so a bare `>` against a billed count throws; an embedded `treatment` carries `@id` but **no bare `id`**; `/prescription_validations` registers `validation` but **silently ignores `passed`** (`validation=17&passed=false` returns the same 2,499 as `validation=17`) and the row **does not carry its prescription**, so there is no way to go from a failing verdict back to a VO; `/activity_treatments` and `/activities` both accept the batched **`prescription[]`** form (5 VOs in ~2 s), which is what makes a census affordable at all. **Coverage stated rather than implied:** staging's Blanko population is `public` 207 / none 43 / `accident` 2 / `private` 1 — **no `privat_basis` Blanko VO**, so one of AC1's four insurance types is unexercised (the fix is an early return before any type is consulted, so it cannot differ by type, but the row is not demonstrated); and Blanko positions exist only for **PT and ERGO**, which is why #3842 AC1 says Logopädie has none. #3842's two FAILING rows (other-area Blanko, non-Blanko) have **no staging fixture either** — 0 of 333 Blanko VOs, against the production copy's 7 and 3. **Mutating**, but narrowly: the only write is `POST /prescriptions/{id}/check-billing-validation`, measured as bounded (verdict rows only, never the VO's queue state), and **VO 6314-2 is in the stale pool and is read-only here by name** — it is #3426's exclusion fixture and must never be posted to. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.blanko-billing-validation.page.ts` |
| `sa_billing_archived_tabs.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @BillingArchivedTabs @ReadOnly` | RC 3.15 (#3835, commits `afe4de25055` api + `22fc8807c28` app on `release/3.15.0`, both `Ref #3835` with **no PR**) invoices of archived VOs must show on every tab of PKV-Abrechnung and Zuzahlungsverwaltung by default, with a new **"Archivierte VOs ausblenden"** checkbox to take them back out. **Deployed on both halves; 10 passed, 0 `fixme`.** A VO auto-archives ~30 days after billing while its invoice stays open, so #3277's rule dropped the invoice off every tab — on the production copy of 25 Sep 2026 **all 254 overdue invoices sat on archived VOs and both "Overdue" tabs showed 0**, which is the tab the billing team chases open invoices from. **Read-only**: every API request is a GET, and the screen is navigated, toggled and selected. **AC5's bulk actions are deliberately NOT run** — they write invoice status, DATEV state and reminder letters — but **AC6's substance needs no write**, because "the action covers only rows the list still shows" is implemented as PRUNING THE SELECTION: selecting a page of the Überfällig tab gives **"10 VO ausgewählt"** and ticking the box leaves **"1 VO ausgewählt"**, bounded by the 5 rows the tab still shows. **THE DEPLOYMENT PROBE HAS TO BE THE NARROWING, NOT A 200:** an unregistered key on these filters is accepted and silently ignored (`pkvBilling[zzzNotAFilter]=true` returns the unfiltered 151), so the test asserts `hideArchived=true` narrows on all three filters (`pkvBilling` 151→29, `copaymentBilling` 397→18, `cancelledRegister` 20→15) **against that bogus-key control**, plus `hideArchived=false` being the default rather than a third behaviour. **The ticket's own Visual Reference is the before/after and it lands exactly** — it predicts the PKV "Überfällig" tab at **119 by default** and "back to today's **5**" when ticked; both measured, on the API and on the painted chip. **AC1's discriminator is the sharpest thing in the file, and it turns on a trap:** `invoice` is `getActiveInvoice()`, which skips cancelled invoices and Stornos (#3535), so **eight** archived copayment VOs serve `invoice: null` — and AC1 lists a VO carrying "an open one, **or only a cancelled one**". Exactly four (8346-2, 8829-1, 9117-1, 9417-1, each with a cancelled copayment plus its Storno) are listed and exactly four (4958-9, 8986-1, 9034-1, 9218-1, no invoice at all) are excluded, so reading that field to decide "has an invoice" reports four correctly-listed VOs as a defect; the real predicate is the filter's own `invoice.id IS NOT NULL OR EXISTS cancelled original`, which from a client means `/invoices?prescription=`. Both halves are required to occur, or the assertion is satisfied by one alone. **AC2's truth table is driven per tab, per page, in both states**, with three clauses that are each a real statement rather than a count: ticking reproduces the **pre-#3835 world exactly** (statuses ⊆ `Fertig Behandelt / Abgerechnet / Abgebrochen`, 0 archived rows); **"Fehler" must NOT move** (1→1 on PKV, 0→0 on copay — an archived VO with an invoice is not on it and one without is on no tab); and **"Alle mit Rechnung" (568→60) and "Storniert" (20→15) newly honour the checkbox**, the latter needing it on its own invoice-rooted request (#3427). **AC3 is the claim that chip == list, so it is asserted on the PAINTED chip against each tab's own query** — 11 chips compared in both states, e.g. unticked `Alle 151 · Alle mit Rechnung 568 · Fehler 1 · Nicht gesendet 19 · Überfällig 119 · Inkasso 2 · An Inkasso gesendet 4 · Bezahlt 2 · Storniert 20` and ticked `29 · 60 · 1 · 18 · 5 · 0 · 1 · 0 · 15`. **THE READINESS TRAP, which fails a correct build:** the chips paint **before their counts land, every one reading `(0)`**, so a gate that merely counts chips returns on a row of zeros and every comparison fails with `Expected 151, Received 0` — reading exactly like the counters being broken (that is how this file first failed; #3337's "0 von 0" again). `waitForChips()` therefore requires enough chips, **at least one non-zero count, and two consecutive identical readings**. **AC4 verified on both surfaces:** on screen the checkbox is **the next labelled control after the VO Status filter** (asserted on x-order over the *labelled* leaves — the raw row also carries the dropdown chevrons as empty leaves and the checkbox's own glyph, so a raw adjacency check reports index 3 where it wants 1 on a correctly placed control), unticked on open, **kept across status tabs**, **each page has its own** (PKV ticked while Zuzahlungsverwaltung opens unticked and shows its own full 397), and **not remembered between visits** — which needs a SECOND BROWSER CONTEXT, since `mintUiSession`'s refresh token is single-use (#3460) and a reload lands on the login form. On the API it **narrows search** (`search[prescriptionId]=7943-3` → 1, ticked → 0; #3277's search-only widening is removed because the default now includes them) and is **inert when "Archiviert" is chosen** (122→122, 383→383 — the explicit `[treatmentStatus]` override keeps precedence). **AC7** holds with archived rows now listed: Zuzahlungsverwaltung is still `["public"]` over 397 rows and PKV-Abrechnung `["private","privat_basis"]` over 151 — the scoping most at risk — and the ticket's own named non-bug is pinned, **VO 6504-1 / R326-46**: archived, GKV, `copaymentLiable: false` because the exemption was entered after the invoice, so it is off the copayment eligibility tabs **by default AND under the Archiviert filter** while still on "Alle mit Rechnung". **MORE TRAPS:** the checkbox has **no `aria-checked`** (`accessibilityState` never reaches the DOM — the RNW gap of #3400 / #3343 / #3505) and no accessible name, and there are 12 `role=checkbox` elements on the page, so its state is the Material glyph — **U+F0131 blank, U+F0132 marked** — and it is located by its label's position; **clicking the LABEL does nothing**, only the box toggles, so a click on the text leaves the glyph, the chips and the requests untouched and reads exactly like an inert checkbox; `invoiceStatus=error` is the "Fehler" tab, while `[error]` and `[withoutInvoice]` are **silently ignored** and return the unfiltered list; and the tab labels differ by page — **German on PKV-Abrechnung, English on Zuzahlungsverwaltung** (`Not Sent / Overdue / Cancelled`), which the Localization Reference states and this ticket does not change. **For the PM:** the Developer Reference proposes `all_with_archived` for the new i18n key; what shipped is **`hide_archived`**, and `all_with_archived` is a *different, pre-existing* key ("Alle inkl. Archivierte", 8 occurrences) — so a probe written from the reference finds a healthy count for the wrong string. **This ticket reverses #3277's AC5 by design**, and `sa_billing_archived_vos.spec.ts` was updated rather than deleted — see its row. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.billing-archived-tabs.page.ts` |
| `sa_duplikat_verloren.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @DuplikatVerloren @ReadOnly` | RC 3.15 (#3722) a VO whose billing deadline passes while still in **Anfordern / Erhalten / Versendet** moves automatically to a new terminal status **Verloren**, and gets its own tile on the Therapeuten-Orga board. **Deployed; 6 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **NO COMMIT ON `release/3.15.0` MENTIONS 3722**, so a commit search reads as "not started" — the verdict is behavioural, from the tile and the status existing at all (the #3895 lesson, where the API half rode in under a performance subject). **Read-only in effect:** two tests POST a status, and both are aimed at a VO ALREADY in the status being posted, so an accepted write would be a no-op (`apply()` returns early on an unchanged status, #3501) and the refusal — the expected outcome — writes nothing either way. **AC1 verified on the whole population:** all **6** Verloren VOs sit strictly past their last billable day (`daysToDeadline` −66 and −5), **and the converse is asserted too** — **0** VOs still in an open status are past theirs, which is what stops "they are all past" being true merely because the sweep has not caught up. **AC3 is the sharpest test, and it is four distinct refusals rather than one:** `POST …/duplikat-status` answers **422 `"Verloren" is set automatically and cannot be chosen.`** — byte-for-byte the shape `Abgerechnet` already had, which IS AC3's "the same restriction that already applies to Abgerechnet", verified side by side — plus **`"Verloren" is final; this VO's Duplikat status can no longer be changed.`** for a move away from it, against the control **`Unknown Duplikat status "NichtEinStatus".`** for a nonsense value. Four different messages is what makes the first two specific rather than "this route refuses everything", and the stored status is re-read afterwards to prove nothing moved. **AC4 on all six:** each carries a `duplikat_status_change` entry reading `Anfordern -> Verloren` **with no author**, stamped at **23:16 UTC** — the `app:duplikat:flag-overdue` nightly (23:15, per #3795's Need Command). **The three newest fixtures came from all three open statuses** (`Anfordern`, `Erhalten`, `Versendet` → Verloren), so AC1's precondition is exercised across its whole range rather than on one. AC2: **0** Verloren rows on the open worklist and none counting toward `duplikatOffen`. AC5: the tile count equals its rows **exactly** (6 = 6) — unlike `duplikatOffen`, whose tile deliberately omits the Abgerechnet row it still lists (the pre-existing asymmetry #3774 recorded, so the two must not be asserted the same way). AC6 plus **#3717's VO-number column** on every row. **AC7 (clicking the tile twice to toggle the drill-down) is not driven** — it is the shared `RiskWorklist` behaviour every Flow Boards tile has, and #3774 `fixme`'d the same click for want of a testid, `role="button"` or `aria-expanded` on the group header. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.duplikat-process.page.ts` (extended with `VERLOREN`, `AUTOMATIC_ONLY_STATUSES` and `DUPLIKAT_TILES`) |
| `sa_refusal_reasons.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @RefusalReasons @ReadOnly` | RC 3.15 (**#3899** commits `d2e9511456a` + `582f91a9b00`, and **#3908** commit `0980fe59cbb`) two defects from the 2026-10-01 Sentry triage, tested in one file because they are the same shape — *a refusal that does not say why* — and share one shipped helper module. **Both deployed; 5 passed, 0 `fixme`.** #3899: five different server rules all reported on `propertyPath: prescription` while the client deliberately sends only field names to Sentry, so **416 events across 101 users** could not be told apart. #3908: the Heilmittel picker offered items already on the session, the API always refused, and the toast said only **"HTTP 422"**. **READ-ONLY IN EFFECT, and the one write is the technique worth keeping:** `POST /activities/bulk` is aimed at a VO that has already used **every** prescribed treatment, so the only correct outcome is a **422** and a passing run creates nothing — the #3549/#3869 reasoning, a write whose refusal IS the assertion. Measured live: `{"propertyPath":"prescription","value":"/prescriptions/5093","message":"This VO has already reached its maximum number of prescribed treatments (10/10)…","code":"ACTIVITY_MAX_TREATMENTS_REACHED"}` — the path unchanged (that ambiguity is the ticket's subject) and the **`code`** now telling the rules apart; the code is asserted **not equal to the message**, because a code echoing the message would satisfy "carries a code" while solving nothing. **AC2 and AC3 are read out of the served bundle VERBATIM**, which is better than inferring them: `violationDigest` returns `{violationCount, violationFields, violationCodes}` and reads **only** `propertyPath` and `code` off a violation — `.message` and `.value` occur nowhere in it, which is AC2's "still never includes violation messages or values" — and it filters codes through **`/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$/`**, which **IS** AC3 ("a code that is not identifier-shaped is dropped"), driven here over both kinds including a German patient name and a 65-character string. The key also sits on the Sentry **`ALLOWLIST_KEYS`** beside `violationFields`/`violationCount`, so the codes survive the scrubber rather than being redacted — not an AC, but the thing that would have made the fix useless. **#3908 AC1 is verified as ORDER, not merely presence:** the shipped callback reads `if(…some(e=>e.treatment?.id===L)) O(F('vo_validation.treatment_history.treatment_already_added'),'error'); else try{…await ae({…})}` — the duplicate check fires and the POST is in the `else`, which is exactly "stopped before any request", with both locales shipping (`Dieses Heilmittel ist bereits in dieser Behandlung enthalten` / `This Heilmittel is already on this treatment`) and the key referenced rather than hardcoded (#3611's failure mode). **AC2: `HTTP 422` occurs 0 times in the whole served bundle** — the string the ticket complains about is gone — with `detailOf` (`body.detail ?? body['hydra:description']`) as the replacement path and a named fallback. **A CONTROL makes those zeros mean something:** the escaping helper is proven able to find a German literal first (`Behandlung erfolgreich hinzugefügt`), because the build escapes non-ASCII and a plain search returns 0 for every German string, reading exactly like "never shipped" (#3337/#3611/#3873). **TRAPS on the bulk endpoint, either of which answers 400 and reads like a refusal on the merits:** the payload is a **bare array** (wrapped in `{activities:[…]}` → *"expected a JSON array"*) and each row needs a **`therapist` IRI** while the VO serializes its therapist as an **object** (→ *"item 0 is missing a therapist IRI"*). **Not covered, with the reason recorded in a green test rather than left implicit:** #3899 AC4/AC5 are a Sentry query after a production release; #3908 AC4/AC5/AC6 concern `app:datev:pull-payments`, a console command whose warning is a CloudWatch log line with **no client surface at all**, plus a PHPUnit allowlist test and a production observation; and #3908 AC3 (adding a Heilmittel that is NOT yet on the session) is a successful write that would put a real `ActivityTreatment` and its price snapshot on a real session. Steps in `docs/manual-test-3899-3908-refusal-reasons.md`. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.refusal-reasons.page.ts` |
| `sa_missing_ik_validation.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @MissingIkValidation @ReadOnly @Mutating @Slow` | RC 3.15 (#3822, commit `23089bebf`, a `Ref` trailer with **no PR**, plus follow-up `0eae51982`) billing validation of a GKV or BG VO must be **refused**, with a message naming the Gesellschaft and the therapy type, when that Gesellschaft has no IK number for the type — before, it succeeded, the VO joined no billing submission and nothing said so (five Curano Stuttgart speech-therapy VOs sat like that on production). **Deployed on both halves; 11 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **EVERY WRITE THIS FILE MAKES IS ONE THE FIX MUST REFUSE, which is what makes a write ticket drivable live:** the check sits in `PrescriptionUpdateProcessor` / `BulkPostValidationStatusController` **before the flush**, so a refusal fires neither the batch listener nor the copayment-invoice listener — the #3899 / #3869 / #3549 shape, where a refusal that ever stops being one IS the defect. **The bulk path is safer still**, because AC3 makes it ALL-OR-NOTHING: an unvalidated **has-IK control VO rides along in the batch** as the discriminator and is still not written (`4363-2`, `updatedAt` unmoved from 2026-06-10). The POSITIVE paths (AC4, AC5) create a submission and a copayment invoice and are therefore read from the PM's 3 Oct footprint rather than re-run. **THE ORACLE, and it is what makes the whole book readable: `ikNumber` is SERVED and IS `PrescriptionPresenter::getIkNumber()`** — the exact value `getMissingIk()` tests — so for a GKV/BG VO with an entity and a therapy type, `ikNumber` absent ⟺ the refusal fires. **TRAP: it is OMITTED WHEN NULL**, i.e. absent precisely on the VOs this ticket is about, which reads as "the field is not served at all" (that conclusion was drawn here, wrongly, from one item read); it is also absent from `/prescriptions/{id}` and `/v2/prescriptions`, and `billing:read` drops `therapyType` / `therapist.therapyDepartment` / `entity.id` (`overrideDefaultGroups`, #3512/#3576), so the three inputs only come back together on the PLAIN `/prescriptions` COLLECTION. The port of `getMissingIk()` is **validated before use — 2,020 validated GKV/BG VOs, 0 disagreements** with the served `ikNumber`, with an anti-vacuity guard requiring both outcomes to occur. Verified: **AC1 row 1 + AC2** the single PATCH refused with the full `MissingIkException` body (`missingIk[]`, `type: /errors/missing-ik`, `title`, the English `detail`) and the VO **byte-unchanged** — status, submission, invoice and even `updatedAt` and the change-log count; **a SECOND refusal at a different Gesellschaft and therapy type** (`100302-1`, Curano Hannover / Physiotherapie against `99969-1`, Berlin-Brandenburg 2 / Logopädie), because one pair cannot tell a message built from the VO from a hardcoded one;
